Z_{t}\in\mathbb{D}^{1,2}
for
\text{a.e.}t\in[0,T]
. By applying the dominated convergence theorem, one can prove that each term in (
3.14
) converges to its corresponding counterpart in equation (
3.10
).

From
[
4
]
the following representation holds:

D_{u}X_{t}^{(i)}=(\nabla_{x^{(i)}}X_{t}^{(i)}))(\nabla_{x^{(i)}}X_{u}^{(i)})^{-1}

for all
0\leq u\leq t\leq T
and for all
0\leq i\leq r
. Furthermore, from the uniqueness of solutions to equation (
3.10
) we also deduce that

D_{u}Y_{t}=\sum_{i=1}^{r}(\nabla_{x^{(i)}}Y_{t})^{\mathbb{T}}(\nabla_{x^{(i)}}X_{u}^{(i)})^{-1}.

Moreover, for each
0\leq v\leq t\leq T
, we have
Y_{t}-Y_{v}=-\int_{v}^{t}g(s,X_{s}^{(i)},Y_{s},Z_{s})\mathrm{d}s+\int_{v}^{t}Z_{s}\mathrm{d}W_{s}
then differentiating both sides of the above equation, we get

D_{u}Y_{t}=-\int_{u}^{t}D_{u}g(s,X_{s}^{(i)},Y_{s},Z_{s})\mathrm{d}s+Z_{u}+\int_{u}^{t}D_{u}Z_{s}\mathrm{d}W_{s}

for all
0\leq v\leq u\leq t\leq T
. In particular, for
u=t
we obtain that

Z_{t}=\sum_{i=1}^{r}(\nabla_{x^{(i)}}Y_{t})^{\mathbb{T}}(\nabla_{x^{(i)}}X_{t}^{(i)})^{-1}.

This conclude the proof.
∎

3.3.
Quadratic BSDEs with discrete functional terminal value

In this subsection, we present some essential properties of solutions to quadratic BSDEs with a discrete functional terminal value. We begin by discussing their differentiability. The following lemma, derived from Propositions
3.4
and
3.6
, is straightforward, so we omit its proof here (for comparison, see
[
54
, Lemma 3.2.8]
).

Lemma 3.7
.

Let us assume that the conditions of Proposition
3.4
are in force except that the terminal value
\xi
is of the form
\xi=\varphi(X_{s_{0}},\cdots,X_{s_{r-1}},X_{s_{r}})
with
0\leq s_{0}\leq s_{1}\leq\cdots\leq s_{r-1}\leq s_{r}=T
, satisfies (
3.7
)–(
3.8
) and
X^{(i)}=X^{(j)}
for all
i,j\in\{1,\cdots,r\}
. Then there exists a unique solution
(\nabla^{j}Y,\nabla^{j}Z)
to the BSDE

\displaystyle\nabla^{j}Y_{t}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i\geq j}^{r}\nabla_{{x^{(i)}}}\varphi(X_{s_{0}},\cdots,X_{s_{r-1}},X_{s_{r}})\nabla_{x}X_{s_{i}}-\int_{t}^{T}\nabla^{j}Z_{s}\mathrm{d}B_{s}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{t}^{T}\langle\nabla g(s,\mathbb{X}_{s}),\nabla_{x}\mathbb{X}_{s}\rangle\mathrm{d}s.

(3.16)

In addition, the following representations hold

\displaystyle D_{u}Y_{t}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{r}y_{i}(t)\left(\nabla_{x}X_{u}\right)^{-1}\mathbb{1}_{\{u\geq s_{i}\}},\quad 0\leq u\leq t,

\displaystyle Z_{t}

\displaystyle=D_{t}Y_{t}=\sum_{i=0}^{r}y_{i}(t)\left(\nabla_{x}X_{t}\right)^{-1}\mathbb{1}_{\{t\geq s_{i}\}},

where the operator
D
denotes the Malliavin derivative.
Moreover, given a fixed time
t
such that
t\in[s_{j},s_{j+1}),0\leq j\leq N
with the convention
[s_{r},s_{r+1})=\{T\}
, we have

D_{u}Y_{t}=\left(\nabla^{j}Y_{t}\right)^{\mathbb{T}}\left(\nabla_{x}X_{u}\right)^{-1},\quad Z_{t}=\left(\nabla^{j}Y_{t}\right)^{\mathbb{T}}\left(\nabla_{x}X_{t}\right)^{-1},

(3.17)

and for all
1\leq i\leq r
,
(y_{0},z_{0})
and
(y_{i},z_{i})\in\mathbb{R}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}
are the adapted solutions to the following linear BSDEs

\displaystyle y_{0}(t)

\displaystyle=-\int_{t}^{T}z_{0}(s)\mathrm{d}W_{s}+\int_{t}^{T}\left(\nabla_{y}g(s,\mathbb{X}_{s})y_{0}(s)+\nabla_{z}g(s,\mathbb{X}_{s})z_{0}(s)\right)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{t}^{T}\nabla_{x}g(s,\mathbb{X}_{s})\nabla{X}_{s}\mathrm{d}s,

(3.18)

\displaystyle y_{i}(t)

\displaystyle=\xi_{i}-\int_{t}^{T}z_{i}(s)\mathrm{d}W_{s}+\int_{t}^{T}\left(\nabla_{y}g(s,\mathbb{X}_{s})y_{i}(s)+\nabla_{z}g(s,\mathbb{X}_{s})z_{i}(s)\right)\mathrm{d}s,

(3.19)

respectively, and
\xi_{i}=\left((\nabla_{x}\varphi)(X_{s_{0}},\cdots,X_{s_{r-1}},X_{s_{r}})\right)^{\mathbb{T}}\nabla_{x}X_{s_{i}}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
.

Remark 3.8
.

From Girsanov’s theorem and the reverse Hölder inequality we have for all
0\leq i\leq r

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}}\Big[\int_{0}^{T}|e_{s}e_{t}^{-1}|^{2}|z_{i}(s)|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big]\leq C(p_{0})\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}[|e_{s}e_{t}^{-1}|^{4p_{0}^{{}^{\prime}}}]+\Big(\int_{0}^{T}|z_{i}(s)|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2p_{0}^{{}^{\prime}}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{p_{0}^{{}^{\prime}}}}<\infty.

Then, the stochastic integral
\int_{0}e_{s}e_{t}^{-1}z_{i}(s)\mathrm{d}W_{s}^{\mathbb{Q}}
defines a true martingale under the probability measure
\mathbb{Q}
.
From the classical linearisation technique, the equations (
3.18
) and (
3.19
) can be rewritten as

\displaystyle y_{0}(t)

\displaystyle=-\int_{t}^{T}e_{s}e_{t}^{-1}z_{0}(s)\mathrm{d}W_{s}^{\mathbb{Q}}+\int_{t}^{T}e_{s}e_{t}^{-1}\nabla_{{x}}g(s,\mathbb{X}_{s})\nabla_{x}{X}_{s}\mathrm{d}s,

(3.20)

\displaystyle y_{i}(t)

\displaystyle=e_{T}e_{t}^{-1}y_{i}(T)-\int_{t}^{T}e_{s}e_{t}^{-1}z_{i}(s)\mathrm{d}W_{s}^{\mathbb{Q}}.

(3.21)

The following lemma links the estimate of a sum of quadratic variation processes to the sum of the terminal values of martingales. A version of this result is provided in
[
53
, Lemma 3.3]
for Lipschitz continuous coefficients. For technical reasons, we will reproduce the version developed in
[
54
, Lemma 3.2.10]

Lemma 3.9
.

Let
y_{i}(t)\in L^{2p}(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{m})
be an
\mathbb{R}^{m}\text{-valued}
adapted martingale for all
0\leq i\leq r
and
p\geq 1
. Then by the martingale representation theorem, there is a square integrable process
z_{i}(t)
such that,
y_{i}(t)=\beta_{i}+\int_{0}^{t}z_{i}(s)\mathrm{d}W_{s}
. For any increasing function
\eta:\{0,1,\cdots,N-1\}\rightarrow\{0,\cdots,r\}
, we define
\tilde{\eta}:\{0,\cdots,r\}\rightarrow\{-1,0,1,\cdots,N-1\}
as
\tilde{\eta}=\sup\{j;\eta(j)\leq i\}
with the convention
\sup\emptyset=-1.
Then, there is a constant
C(p,m)
independent of
r
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}\Big|\sum_{i=\eta(j)}^{r}z_{i}(s)\Big|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{p}\Big]\leq C(p,m)\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\sum_{i=0}^{r}y_{i}(t_{\tilde{\eta}(i)+1})-y_{i}(0)\Big|^{2p}\Big].

The following result addresses the integrability of the supremum norm of the process
Z
, which is crucial for proving the main results of this paper. Its extension to the case of functional terminal values will be discussed in Remark
3.11
.

Lemma 3.10
.

Let assumptions of Lemma
3.7
hold. Then for any
p>p_{0}^{\prime}>1
there is a constant
C
only depending on
p,d,T
and
\|b\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T];C_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}
such that

\mathbb{E}\big[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|Z_{t}|^{p}\big]\leq C(1+S^{p}({\varphi})),

(3.22)

where
p_{0}^{\prime}
is the conjugate of
p_{0}
and
p_{0}
is such that
\mathscr{E}\left(\nabla_{z}g*W\right)\in L^{p_{0}}
(see Remark
3.5
).
In particular, if
g
is Lipschitz continuous in
y
then the control process
Z
is uniformly bounded i.e. there is a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle|Z_{t}|\leq Ce^{\|\nabla_{y}g\|_{\infty}}(1+S(\varphi)).

Proof.

First, we assume that the coefficients
b,g
and
\xi
are continuously differentiable and the terminal value
\xi
is of the form
\xi=\varphi(X_{s_{0}},\cdots,X_{s_{r-1}},X_{s_{r}})
with
0\leq s_{0}\leq s_{1}\leq\cdots\leq s_{r-1}\leq s_{r}=T
, satisfying (
3.7
)-(
3.8
). From Proposition
3.4
, the solution
(Y,Z)
is differentiable, and the derivative process
(\nabla^{j}Y,\nabla^{j}Z)
satisfies the linear BSDE (
3.16
) for all
1\leq j\leq r
. To derive the desired estimate, it is enough to establish a bound for
|(\nabla^{j}Y_{t})^{\mathbb{T}}(\nabla X_{t})^{-1}|
for all
1\leq j\leq r
. Given the assumption on the driver
g
, we have,
|\nabla_{z}g(s,\mathbb{X}_{s})|\leq\Lambda_{z}(1+4\ell(|Y_{s}|)|Z_{s}|)\in\mathscr{H}_{BMO}.
Using a standard linearisation technique and applying Itô’s formula, we deduce

\displaystyle e_{t}\nabla^{j}Y_{t}

\displaystyle=e_{T}\sum_{i=j}^{r}\nabla_{{x^{(i)}}}\varphi(X_{s_{0}},\cdots,X_{s_{r-1}},X_{s_{r}})\nabla_{x}X_{s_{i}}-\int_{t}^{T}e_{s}\nabla Z_{s}\mathrm{d}W_{s}^{\mathbb{Q}}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{t}^{T}e_{s}\nabla_{x}g(s,{X}_{s},Y_{s},Z_{s})\cdot\nabla{X}_{s}\mathrm{d}s,

(3.23)

where the measure
\mathbb{Q}
with its associated Radon-Nikodym derivative
\mathrm{d}\mathbb{Q}:=\mathscr{E}(\nabla_{z}g*W)\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}
and
W^{\mathbb{Q}}
are all defined in Remark
3.5
.
By taking the conditional expectation in (
3.23
)
and using the fact that the process
(\nabla{X}_{t})^{-1}
is
\mathfrak{F}_{t}\text{-measurable}
we obtain that

\displaystyle\big|\nabla^{j}Y_{t}(\nabla{X}_{t})^{-1}\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|(\nabla{X}_{t})^{-1}|\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}}\Big[A_{T}\Big(\big|\sum_{i=j}^{r}\nabla_{{x^{(i)}}}\varphi(X_{s_{0}},\cdots,X_{s_{r-1}},X_{s_{r}})\nabla_{x}X_{s_{i}}\big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\int_{t}^{T}\nabla_{x}g(s,{X}_{s},Y_{s},Z_{s})\cdot\nabla{X}_{s}\mathrm{d}s\Big|\Big)\Big/\mathfrak{F}_{t}\Big]

Using
p_{0}^{\prime}\in(1,p)
we obtain from the reverse Hölder inequality the existence of a constant
C
depending on
p_{0}
with
1/p_{0}+1/p_{0}^{{}^{\prime}}=1
such that

\displaystyle\big|\nabla^{j}Y_{t}(\nabla{X}_{t})^{-1}\big|

\displaystyle\leq C(p_{0})|(\nabla{X}_{t})^{-1}|\mathbb{E}\Big[A_{T}^{p_{0}^{\prime}}\Big(\Big|\sum_{i=j}^{r}\nabla_{{x^{(i)}}}\varphi(X_{s_{0}},\cdots,X_{s_{r-1}},X_{s_{r}})\nabla_{x}X_{s_{i}}\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+\Big|\int_{t}^{T}\nabla_{x}g(s,{X}_{s},Y_{s},Z_{s})\cdot\nabla{X}_{s}\mathrm{d}s\Big|\Big)^{p_{0}^{\prime}}\Big/\mathfrak{F}_{t}\Big]^{\frac{1}{p_{0}^{\prime}}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\big|\nabla^{j}Y_{t}(\nabla{X}_{t})^{-1}\big|^{p}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C(p,p_{0}^{\prime})\mathbb{E}[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|(\nabla{X}_{t})^{-1}|^{2p}]+C(p,p_{0}^{\prime})\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\mathbb{E}\Big[A_{T}^{p_{0}^{\prime}}\Big(\Big|\sum_{i=j}^{r}\nabla_{{x^{(i)}}}\varphi(X_{s_{0}},\cdots,X_{s_{r-1}},X_{s_{r}})\nabla_{x}X_{s_{i}}\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+\Big|\int_{t}^{T}\nabla_{x}g(s,{X}_{s},Y_{s},Z_{s})\cdot\nabla{X}_{s}\mathrm{d}s\Big|\Big)^{p_{0}^{\prime}}\Big/\mathfrak{F}_{t}\Big]^{\frac{2}{p_{0}^{\prime}}}\Bigg]^{p}

Successive application of the Doob’s maximal and Hölder’s inequalities gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\big|\nabla^{j}Y_{t}(\nabla{X}_{t})^{-1}\big|^{p}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C(p,p_{0}^{\prime})\mathbb{E}[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|(\nabla{X}_{t})^{-1}|^{2p}]+C(p^{\prime}_{0},p_{0},p)\mathbb{E}\Big[A_{T}^{p}\Big\{\Big|\sum_{i=j}^{r}\nabla_{{x^{(i)}}}\varphi(X_{s_{0}},\cdots,X_{s_{r-1}},X_{s_{r}})\nabla_{x}X_{s_{i}}\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\nabla_{x}g(s,{X}_{s},Y_{s},Z_{s})\cdot\nabla{X}_{s}\mathrm{d}s\Big|\Big\}^{2p}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C(p,p_{0}^{\prime})\mathbb{E}[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|(\nabla{X}_{t})^{-1}|^{2p}]+C(p^{\prime}_{0},p_{0},p)\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big\{\Big|\sum_{i=j}^{r}\nabla_{{x^{(i)}}}\varphi(X_{s_{0}},\cdots,X_{s_{r-1}},X_{s_{r}})\nabla_{x}X_{s_{i}}\Big|

\displaystyle\quad+\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\nabla_{x}g(s,{X}_{s},Y_{s},Z_{s})\cdot\nabla{X}_{s}\mathrm{d}s\Big|\Big\}^{4p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

By using the growth of
g
we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\big|\nabla^{j}Y_{t}(\nabla{X}_{t})^{-1}\big|^{p}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C(p,p_{0}^{\prime})\mathbb{E}[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|(\nabla{X}_{t})^{-1}|^{2p}]+C(p^{\prime}_{0},p_{0},p)\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\sum_{i=j}^{r}\nabla_{{x^{(i)}}}\varphi(X_{s_{0}},\cdots,X_{s_{r-1}},X_{s_{r}})\nabla_{x}X_{s_{i}}\Big|^{4p}

\displaystyle\quad+\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}|\nabla{X}_{s}|^{8p}+\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\Lambda_{x}(1+|Y_{s}|+\ell(|Y_{s}|)|Z_{s}|^{\alpha})\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{8p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(p^{\prime}_{0},p_{0},p,T,\|Z*W\|_{\operatorname{BMO}},\|b\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T];C_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))})(1+S^{p}(\varphi)),

(3.24)

where we used Theorem
2.4
, equation (
2.6
), the boundedness of
Y
and the energy inequality to obtain the last inequality. By applying a standard approximation procedure, one can show that the bound remains valid even when the coefficients are not differentiable.

For the second point, if
g
is Lipschitz continuous in
y
then the process
A_{T}
is uniformly bounded by
e^{\|\nabla_{y}g\|_{\infty}}
. There exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\big|\nabla^{j}Y_{t}(\nabla{X}_{t})^{-1}\big|

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{\|\nabla_{y}g\|_{\infty}}\Big(S(\varphi)+\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}}\Big[\int_{t}^{T}(1+\ell(|Y_{s}|)|Z_{s}|^{\alpha})^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big/\mathfrak{F}_{t}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}}\Big[\sup_{t\leq s\leq T}|\nabla{X}_{s}(\nabla{X}_{t})^{-1}|^{2}\Big/\mathfrak{F}_{t}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{\|\nabla_{y}g\|_{\infty}}(1+S(\varphi)),

where we applied the fact that
Y
is bounded, the energy’s inequality and Proposition
2.6
to obtain the last inequality,
C>0
is a constant only depending on T and
\|Z*W\|_{\operatorname{BMO}}
and
\|b\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T];C_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}
.
This concludes the proof.
∎

Remark 3.11
.

•

The above lemma remains valid for the general case when
\xi=\Phi(X.)
by using standard stability arguments of BSDEs and the Fatou’s Lemma. This is achieved by considering the bounded smooth approximation
\{\hat{\varphi}^{\pi}\}_{\pi}
of
\Phi(X)
(as discussed in Remark
3.3
), with the partition
\{\pi\}
of
[0,T]
, satisfying
|\pi|\rightarrow 0
and choosing
S(\varphi)<C\Lambda_{\Phi}
. In other words, for any
p>p_{0}^{\prime}>1
there exists a constant
C
depending only on
p,d,T
and
\|b\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T];C_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))}
such that

\mathbb{E}\big[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|Z_{t}|^{p}\big]\leq C(1+\Lambda^{p}_{\Phi}),

(3.25)

where
p_{0}^{\prime}
is the conjugate of
p_{0}
and
p_{0}
is such that
\mathscr{E}\left(\nabla_{z}g*W\right)\in L^{p_{0}}
(see Remark
3.5
).

•

Similarly, for
\xi=\Phi(X.)
and if
g
is uniformly Lipschitz continuous in
y
, we obtain the following uniform bound of
Z_{t}
for all
t\in[0,T]

|Z_{t}|\leq Ce^{\|\nabla_{y}g\|_{\infty}}(1+\Lambda_{\Phi}).

(3.26)

•

In the context of path-dependent FBSDE, the result above was first derived in
[
53
, Lemma 3.2]
under the Cauchy-Lipschitz framework with an additional
C^{1/2,1}
assumption on the coefficients. Recently,
[
54
, Lemma 3.2.7]
, extended this to quadratic FBSDEs with
(t,y,z)\mapsto g(t,y,z)\in C^{1/2,1,1}
and further assumed that the drift
b\in C^{1+\alpha}
for some
\alpha>0
. Beyond the
L^{\infty}\text{-Lipschitz}
(or
L^{1}\text{-Lipschitz}
) condition on the terminal value, the current result applies to quadratic FBSDEs with non-differentiable coefficients and non-smooth drift.

3.4.
Representation theorem revisited

In this subsection, we assume that the diffusion process
X
in (
1.1
) starts at
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
at a fixed time
s\in[0,T]
, and we consider the Brownian filtration
\mathfrak{F}_{t}^{s}=\sigma(W_{u}-W_{s},s\leq u\leq t)
with the usual
\mathbb{P}
augmentation. We further assume that the coefficients
b
and
g
satisfy Assumption
1.1
, and that the terminal value
\xi
is of the form
\xi=\varphi(X_{t_{1}},X_{t_{2}},\ldots,X_{t_{n}})
, where
\varphi
is any bounded and Lipschitz continuous function.

Theorem 3.12
.

Suppose
\xi=\varphi(X_{t_{1}},X_{t_{2}},\ldots,X_{t_{n}})
, satisfies (
3.7
)–(
3.8
), where
t\leq t_{1}<t_{2}<\ldots<t_{n}\leq T
is any partition of
[t,T]
. Suppose Assumption
1.1
holds and let
(X,Y,Z)
be the solution to the corresponding FBSDE (
1.1
)-(
1.2
). Then, on each interval
(t_{i-1},t_{i}),i=1,\ldots,n
the following holds
\mathbb{P}\text{-almost surely}

\displaystyle Z_{t}=\mathbb{E}\Big[\varphi(X_{t_{1}},X_{t_{2}},\cdots,X_{t_{n}})N_{t_{i}}^{t}+\int_{t}^{T}g(v,X_{v},Y_{v},Z_{v})N_{v\wedge t_{i}}^{t}\mathrm{d}v\Big/\mathfrak{F}_{t}^{s}\Big],

(3.27)

and

N^{t}_{v}=\frac{1}{v-t}(M_{v}^{t})^{\mathbb{T}}(\nabla X_{t})^{-1},\quad 0\leq s\leq t<v\leq T,

(3.28)

and the two parameters process
M_{v}^{s}
is given by

M_{v}^{t}=\int_{t}^{v}(\nabla X_{\tau})^{\mathbb{T}}\mathrm{d}W_{\tau}.

(3.29)

\nabla X=(\nabla^{1}X,\cdots,\nabla^{d}X)
stands for the first variation of the diffusion
X
as defined in Definition
2.2
. There exists a version of
Z
that satisfy the following properties

(i)

the mapping
t\mapsto Z_{t}
is a.s. continuous on each interval
(t_{i-1},t_{i})
,
i=1,\cdots,n
;

(ii)

both limits
Z_{t_{i}-}:=\lim_{s\uparrow t_{i}}Z_{s}
and
Z_{t_{i}+}:=\lim_{s\downarrow t_{i}}Z_{s}
exist;

(iii)

for all
p>0
, there is a constant
C_{p}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}|\Delta Z_{t_{i}}|^{p}\leq C_{p}.

(3.30)

Consequently, the process
Z
has both càdlàg and càglàd versions, with discontinuities
t_{0},\ldots,t_{n}
and jump sizes satisfying (
3.30
).

The proof of this theorem can be carried out using standard approximation techniques similar to those in
[
21
]
. We will not reproduce the details here.
We conclude this section by recalling the following useful result.

Lemma 3.13
.

Let
T>0
be fixed and suppose that
X
and
Y
are two processes such that
X
is a square integrable martingale and
Y
is also square integrable. Then the following holds

\mathbb{E}\left[X_{t}Y_{t}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[X_{T}Y_{t}\right],\text{ for all }0\leq t\leq T.

(3.31)

4.
Proof of the main results

This section is devoted to the proofs of the main results.

4.1.
Proofs of Theorem
1.4

This section is dedicated to the proofs of Theorems
1.4
,
1.5
, and Proposition
1.6
, below. As previously mentioned, the proof of Theorem
1.4
follows from Theorem
1.5
using approximation and stability results for quadratic BSDEs. One of the challenges is to find an appropriate approximation for the path-dependent terminal value
\Phi
, as outlined for instance in Remark
3.3
.

Proof of Theorem
1.5
.

As in the proof of Lemma
3.10
, we will first assume that the coefficients
b,g,\xi
are continuously differentiable.
We recall that for all
s\leq v\leq t
,

Y_{s}=\xi+\int_{s}^{t}g(v,X_{v},Y_{v},Z_{v})\mathrm{d}v-\int_{s}^{t}Z_{v}\mathrm{d}B_{v}.

Then from BDG inequality we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{s\leq r\leq t}|Y_{r}-Y_{s}|^{p}\Big]\leq C(p)\Big\{\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{s}^{t}|g(v,X_{v},Y_{v},Z_{v})|\mathrm{d}v\Big)^{p}+\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{s}^{t}|Z_{v}|^{2}\mathrm{d}v\Big)^{p/2}\Big\}.

By using the growth of
g
and the fact that
Y_{v}
is bounded we obtain that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{s\leq r\leq t}|Y_{r}-Y_{s}|^{p}\Big]\leq C(p)\Big\{|t-s|^{p}+\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\int_{s}^{t}(1+\ell(|Y_{v}|))|Z_{v}|^{2}\mathrm{d}v\Big)^{p}+\Big(\int_{s}^{t}|Z_{v}|^{2}\mathrm{d}v\Big)^{p/2}\Big]\Big\}.

From to the local boundedness of
\ell
and the bound (
3.22
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{s\leq r\leq t}|Y_{r}-Y_{s}|^{p}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(p)\Big\{|t-s|^{p}+|t-s|^{p}\mathbb{E}[\sup_{s\leq v\leq t}|Z_{v}|^{2p}]+|t-s|^{p/2}\mathbb{E}[\sup_{s\leq v\leq t}|Z_{v}|^{2p}]\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(p)(1+S(\varphi)^{2p})\Big\{|t-s|^{p}+|t-s|^{p/2}\Big\}.

Let us focus now on the regularity of the process
Z
. We recall that our main aim is to establish the following bound

\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|Z_{t}-Z_{t_{j}}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\Big)^{p}\leq Ch^{p},

where
\pi_{0}:0=s_{0}<\cdots<s_{r-1}<s_{r}=T
is a partition of
[0,T]
,
and
\pi_{1}:0=t_{0}<t_{1}<\cdots<t_{N}=T
is another partition of
[0,T]
and
h=\sup_{j}|t_{j+1}-t_{j}|
.
From Jensen’s inequality,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|Z_{t}-Z_{t_{j}}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\Big)^{p}\leq Ch^{p-1}\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\mathbb{E}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|Z_{t}-Z_{t_{j}}|^{2p}\mathrm{d}t.

Thus, it is sufficient to evaluate the quantity
\mathbb{E}|Z_{t}-Z_{t_{j}}|^{2p}
, for all
p>1
. We will establish the result for the case where
\pi_{0}
is finer than
\pi_{1}
; the converse can be handled similarly without significant difficulty (see
[
46
]
). Assume
t_{i}=s_{l_{i}}
for some increasing sequence
(l_{i})_{i=1}^{N}
. For technical reasons, we will use of the expressions provided by equation (
3.17
). Specifically, for any
t\in[s_{k},s_{k+1})\subset[s_{l_{j}},s_{l_{j+1}})=[t_{j},t_{j+1})
, we write

\displaystyle Z_{t}-Z_{t_{j}}

\displaystyle=\left(\nabla^{k}Y_{t}\right)^{\mathbb{T}}\left(\nabla_{x}X_{t}\right)^{-1}-\left(\nabla^{l_{j}}Y_{t_{j}}\right)^{\mathbb{T}}\left(\nabla_{x}X_{t_{j}}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=I_{t}^{1,j}+I_{t}^{2,j},

where
I_{t}^{1,j}:=\left(\nabla^{l_{j}}Y_{t}\right)^{\mathbb{T}}\left((\nabla_{x}X_{t})^{-1}-(\nabla_{x}X_{t_{j}})^{-1}\right)
and
I_{t}^{2,j}:=\left(\nabla^{k}Y_{t}-\nabla^{l_{j}}Y_{t_{j}}\right)^{\mathbb{T}}\left(\nabla_{x}X_{t_{j}}\right)^{-1}
. Thus,

\displaystyle C_{p}h^{p-1}\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|Z_{t}-Z_{t_{j}}|^{2p}\mathrm{d}t\Big]\leq C_{p}h^{p-1}\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}\left(|I_{t}^{1,j}|^{2p}+|I_{t}^{2,j}|^{2p}\right)\mathrm{d}t\Big].

The contribution of
\mathbb{E}[|I_{t}^{1,j}|^{2p}]
can be derived similarly to the approach in
[
21
, Page 4786]
. For completeness, we briefly reproduce it here. Since
b
is assumed to be continuously differentiable,
the map
t\mapsto(\nabla_{x}X_{t})^{-1}
satisfies a linear equation. Therefore, for all
0\leq t_{j}\leq t
one can write

\displaystyle|(\nabla_{x}X_{t})^{-1}-(\nabla_{x}X_{t_{j}})^{-1}|

\displaystyle=\Big|\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{t_{j}<t_{j_{1}}<\ldots<t_{j_{k}}<t}b^{\prime}(t_{j_{1}},X_{t_{j_{1}}}^{x}):\cdots:b^{\prime}(t_{j_{k}},X_{t_{j_{k}}}^{x})\mathrm{d}t_{j_{1}}\cdots\mathrm{d}t_{j_{k}}\Big|,

(4.1)

where the symbol
``:"
stands for the matrix multiplication.
For any
p\geq 2
, Hölder’s inequality and (
3.17
) yield

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|I_{t}^{1,j}|^{2p}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|\nabla_{x}X_{t}|^{8p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|Z_{t}|^{8p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathbb{E}\Big[|(\nabla_{x}X_{t})^{-1}-(\nabla_{x}X_{t_{j}})^{-1}|^{4p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Substitute (
4.1
) into the above inequality and apply Theorem
2.4
, the bound (
3.22
), and Hölder’s inequality again to obtain with
h_{j}=|t_{j+1}-t_{j}|

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|I_{t}^{1,j}|^{2p}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|\nabla_{x}X_{t}|^{8p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|Z_{t}|^{8p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{t_{j}<t_{j_{1}}<\ldots<t_{j_{k}}<t}b^{\prime}(t_{j_{1}},X_{t_{j_{1}}}):\cdots:b^{\prime}(t_{j_{k}},X_{t_{j_{k}}})\mathrm{d}t_{j_{1}}\cdots\mathrm{d}t_{j_{k}}\Big|^{4p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(1+\|b\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T];C_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))})(1+S(\varphi)^{2p})

\displaystyle\quad\times\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\int_{t_{j}<t_{j_{1}}<\ldots<t_{j_{k}}<t}b^{\prime}(t_{j_{1}},X_{t_{j_{1}}}):\cdots:b^{\prime}(t_{j_{k}},X_{t_{j_{k}}})\mathrm{d}t_{j_{1}}\cdots\mathrm{d}t_{j_{k}}\Big|^{4p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(1+\|b\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T];C_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))})(1+S(\varphi)^{2p})h_{j}^{p},

(4.2)

where, we used Girsanov’s theorem and
[
38
, Proposition 3.7]
to derive the last inequality. We emphasize that the constant above does not depend on the Lipschitz norm of the drift
b
, unlike in
[
53
]
or
[
54
]
.
Let us turn now to the control of
I^{2,j}_{t}
.
From Lemma
3.7
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\nabla^{k}Y_{t}-\nabla^{l_{j}}Y_{t_{j}}=y_{0}(t)-y_{0}(t_{j})+\sum_{i=k}^{r}\big(y_{i}(t)-y_{i}(t_{j})\big)-\sum_{i\geq l_{j}}^{k-1}y_{i}(t_{j}).

(4.3)

Using the mean value theorem, Remark
3.8
and the definition of
W^{\mathbb{Q}}
, we observe that for
\tau\in[t_{j},t]

\displaystyle y_{i}(t)-y_{i}(t_{j})

\displaystyle=e_{t_{j}}^{-1}\left[\big(e_{t}y_{i}(t)-e_{t_{j}}y_{i}(t_{j})\big)+(e_{t_{j}}-e_{t})y_{i}(t)\right]

\displaystyle=-(t-t_{j})\nabla_{y}g(\tau,\mathbb{X}_{\tau})e_{\tau}e_{t_{j}}^{-1}y_{i}(t_{j})+\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t}e_{\tau}e_{t_{j}}^{-1}z_{i}({\tau})\mathrm{d}W_{\tau}

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t}e_{\tau}e_{t_{j}}^{-1}\nabla_{z}g({\tau},\mathbb{X}_{\tau})z_{i}({\tau})\mathrm{d}{\tau}.

Similarly, for
\tau\in[t_{j},t]

\displaystyle y_{0}(t)-y_{0}(t_{j})

\displaystyle=\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t}e_{\tau}e_{t_{j}}^{-1}z_{0}({\tau})\mathrm{d}W_{\tau}-\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t}e_{\tau}e_{t_{j}}^{-1}\nabla_{x}g({\tau},\mathbb{X}_{\tau})\nabla X_{\tau}\mathrm{d}{\tau}

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t}e_{\tau}e_{t_{j}}^{-1}z_{0}({\tau})\nabla_{z}g({\tau},\mathbb{X}_{\tau})\mathrm{d}{\tau}.

Thus, (
4.3
) becomes

\displaystyle\nabla^{k}Y_{t}-\nabla^{l_{j}}Y_{t_{j}}

\displaystyle=-e_{\tau}e_{t_{j}}^{-1}\nabla_{y}g(\tau,\mathbb{X}_{\tau})(t-t_{j})\sum_{i=k}^{r}y_{i}(t_{j})-\sum_{i\geq l_{j}}^{k-1}y_{i}(t_{j})

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{i=k}^{r}\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t}e_{\tau}e_{t_{j}}^{-1}\nabla_{{z}}g({\tau},\mathbb{X}_{\tau})z_{i}({\tau})\mathrm{d}{\tau}-\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t}e_{\tau}e_{t_{j}}^{-1}z_{i}({\tau})\mathrm{d}W_{\tau}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t}e_{\tau}e_{t_{j}}^{-1}\nabla_{{x}}g({\tau},\mathbb{X}_{\tau})\nabla_{x}{X}_{\tau}\mathrm{d}{\tau}-\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t}e_{\tau}e_{t_{j}}^{-1}\nabla_{{z}}g({\tau},\mathbb{X}_{\tau})z_{0}({\tau})\mathrm{d}{\tau}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t}e_{\tau}e_{t_{j}}^{-1}z_{0}({\tau})\mathrm{d}W_{\tau}.

(4.4)

Observe that the process
e_{t_{j}}=\exp(\int_{0}^{t_{j}}\Lambda_{y}(1+|Z_{u}|^{\alpha_{0}})\mathrm{d}u)
is bounded from below by one. Then by taking the
p\text{-th}
power, then the supremum, and applying the conditional BDG inequality, we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|\nabla^{k}Y_{t}-\nabla^{l_{j}}Y_{t_{j}}\big|^{2p}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{p}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|(t_{j+1}-t_{j})\nabla_{y}g(\tau,\mathbb{X}_{\tau})\sum_{i=k}^{r}e_{\tau}y_{i}(t_{j})\Big|^{2p}+\Big|\sum_{i\geq l_{j}}^{k-1}e_{t_{j}}y_{i}(t_{j})\Big|^{2p}

\displaystyle+\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t}\sum_{i\geq k}^{r}e_{\tau}\nabla_{{z}}g({\tau},\mathbb{X}_{\tau})z_{i}({\tau})\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{2p}+\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}\Big|\sum_{i\geq k}^{r}e_{\tau}z_{i}({\tau})\Big|^{2}\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{p}

\displaystyle+\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}\big|e_{\tau}z_{0}({\tau})\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{p}+\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}|e_{\tau}\nabla_{{x}}g({\tau},\mathbb{X}_{\tau})\nabla_{x}{X}_{\tau}|\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{2p}

\displaystyle\quad+\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}|e_{\tau}\nabla_{{z}}g({\tau},\mathbb{X}_{\tau})z_{0}({\tau})|\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{2p}\Big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}\Big].

(4.5)

Let us recall that the expression of
I_{t}^{2,j}
is given by
I_{t}^{2,j}:=\left(\nabla^{k}Y_{t}-\nabla^{l_{j}}Y_{t_{j}}\right)^{\mathbb{T}}\left(\nabla_{x}X_{t_{j}}\right)^{-1}
and using the fact that the process
(\nabla_{x}{X}_{t_{j}})^{-1}
is
\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}\text{-measurable}
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}\big|I_{t}^{2,j}\big|^{2p}\mathrm{d}t\Big]\leq\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\sum_{k=l_{j}}^{l_{j+1}-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{s_{k}}^{s_{k+1}}\mathbb{E}\big[\big|\nabla^{k}Y_{t}-\nabla^{l_{j}}Y_{t_{j}}\big|^{2p}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}\big]|(\nabla X_{t_{j}})^{-1}|^{2p}\mathrm{d}t\Big],

(4.6)

which can be written as
\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}\big|I_{t}^{2,j}\big|^{2p}\mathrm{d}t\Big]\leq C_{p}\sum_{\nu=1}^{7}T_{\nu},
and each term
T_{\nu}
for
1\leq\nu\leq 7
comes from (
4.5
).

We introduce another partition
\pi_{2}
finer than
\pi_{1}
defined by:
\pi_{2}:0=\mu_{0}<\mu_{1}<\cdots<\mu_{\hat{\varrho}_{1}}<\cdots<\mu_{\hat{\varrho}_{2}}<\cdots\mu_{\hat{\varrho}_{N}}=T
and satisfying the following properties (see
[
54
,
53
]
).

•

\mu_{\hat{\varrho}_{i}}=s_{l_{i}}=t_{i}
with
\hat{\varrho}_{i+1}-\hat{\varrho}_{i}=\gamma
for some
\gamma>0
large enough, for all
0\leq i\leq r
;

•

there exists
\varrho_{k}=\frac{\mu_{\hat{\varrho}_{i}+k+1}-\mu_{\hat{\varrho}_{i}+k}}{t_{i+1}-t_{i}}
independent of
i
for all
0\leq k\leq\gamma-1
such that
\sum_{k=0}^{\gamma-1}\varrho_{k}=1
;

•

\pi_{1}
belongs to
\pi_{2}
and there exist a surjective function
\eta(i,k):\{0,\cdots,N-1\}\times\{0,\cdots,\gamma\}\rightarrow\{0,1,\cdots,r\}
such that
\mu_{\hat{\varrho}_{i}+k}=s_{\eta(i,k)}
for all
0\leq i\leq N-1
and
0\leq k\leq\gamma-1
;

•

l_{i}\leq\eta(i,k)\leq l_{j+1}-1
with
\eta(i,k+1)\geq\eta(i,k)
,
\eta(i+1,k)\geq\eta(i,\iota)
for all
i,k,\iota
.

Hence, we observe for instance with the term
T_{4}
that

\displaystyle T_{4}=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\sum_{k=l_{j}}^{l_{j+1}-1}(s_{k+1}-{s_{k}})\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}\Big|\sum_{i=k}^{r}e_{\tau}z_{i}({\tau})\Big|^{2}\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{p}\big|(\nabla X_{t_{j}})^{-1}\big|^{2p}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\sum_{k=0}^{\gamma-1}\frac{(\mu_{k+1}-{\mu_{k}})}{t_{j+1}-t_{j}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}\Big|\sum_{i=\eta(j,k)}^{r}e_{\tau}z_{i}({\tau})\Big|^{2}\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{p}\big|(\nabla X_{t_{j}})^{-1}\big|^{2p}\Big]h_{j}.

Thus
T_{4}
can be estimated as follows

\displaystyle T_{4}\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{\gamma-1}\varrho_{k}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}\Big|\sum_{i=\eta(j,k)}^{r}e_{\tau}z_{i}({\tau})\Big|^{2}\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{p}\big|(\nabla X_{t_{j}})^{-1}\big|^{2p}\Big]h

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{\gamma-1}\varrho_{k}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}\Big|\sum_{i=\eta(j,k)}^{r}e_{\tau}z_{i}({\tau})\Big|^{2}\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{2p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{j}\big|(\nabla X_{t_{j}})^{-1}\big|^{4p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}h.

(4.7)

Note that
\mathbb{E}[\sup_{j}|(\nabla X_{t_{j}})^{-1}|^{4p}]<C(1+\|b\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T];C_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))})
, thanks to Proposition
2.6
.
On the other hand, by first applying the reverse Hölder inequality and then using the Lemma
3.9
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}\Big|\sum_{i=\eta(j,k)}^{r}e_{\tau}z_{i}({\tau})\Big|^{2}\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{2p}\Big]\leq

\displaystyle C(p_{1})\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}}\Big[\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}\Big|\sum_{i=\eta(j,k)}^{r}e_{\tau}z_{i}({\tau})\Big|^{2}\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{2pp_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{p_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(p,p_{1})\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}}\Big[\Big|\sum_{i=0}^{r}e_{t_{\tilde{\eta}(j,k)+1}}^{-1}y_{i}(t_{\tilde{\eta}(j,k)+1})-e_{0}^{-1}y_{i}(0)\Big|^{4pp_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{p_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}}}.

Using the expression of
y_{i}
given by (
3.21
) in Remark
3.8
under the probability measure
\mathbb{Q}
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}\Big|\sum_{i=\eta(j,k)}^{r}e_{\tau}z_{i}({\tau})\Big|^{2}\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{2p}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(p,p_{1})\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}}\Big[\Big|\sum_{i=0}^{r}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}}\big[e_{T}y_{i}(T)/\mathfrak{F}_{(t_{\tilde{\eta}(j,k)+1})}\big]\Big|^{4pp_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}}+\Big|\sum_{i=0}^{r}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}}[e_{T}y_{i}(T)]\Big|^{4pp_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{p_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(p,p_{1})\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}}\Big[S(\varphi)^{4pp_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}}\Big(\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}}\big[e_{T}\sup_{0\leq s_{i}\leq T}|\nabla_{x}X_{s_{i}}|/\mathfrak{F}_{s}\big]\Big)^{4pp_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle+S(\varphi)^{4pp_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}}\Big[|e_{T}\sup_{0\leq s_{i}\leq T}|\nabla_{x}X_{s_{i}}|\Big]^{4pp_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{p_{1}^{{}^{\prime}}}},

where the last inequality follows from (
3.21
) in Remark
3.8
, the definition of the terminal value and noting that
S(\varphi)=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\Lambda_{\varphi}^{i}
denotes the sum of the Lipschitz constant of the functional
\varphi
.
By applying the Doob’s maximal inequality and the reverse Hölder’s inequality we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}\Big|\sum_{i=\eta(j,k)}^{r}e_{\tau}z_{i}(\tau)\Big|^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau\Big)^{2p}\Big]\leq

\displaystyle C(p,p_{1},p_{1}^{\prime})\Big(S(\varphi)^{4p}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}}\big[e_{T}^{4p}\sup_{0\leq s_{i}\leq T}|\nabla_{x}X_{s_{i}}|^{4p}\big]\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(p,p_{1},p_{1}^{\prime})C(p_{0})\Big(S(\varphi)^{4p}\mathbb{E}\big[e_{T}^{4pp_{0}^{\prime}}\sup_{0\leq s_{i}\leq T}|\nabla_{x}X_{s_{i}}|^{4pp_{0}^{\prime}}\big]^{\frac{1}{p_{0}^{\prime}}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(1+\|b\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T];C_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))})S(\varphi)^{4p},

(4.8)

where, the constant
C
depends only on
p,p_{1},p_{1}^{\prime},p_{0}^{\prime}
and the BMO norm of
Z
. Hence, by plugging (
4.8
) in (
4.7
) we obtain an bound for the term
T_{4}
which is given by:

\displaystyle T_{4}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{k=0}^{\gamma-1}\varrho_{k}(1+\|b\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T];C_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))})S(\varphi)^{2p}h

\displaystyle\leq C(1+\|b\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T];C_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))})S(\varphi)^{2p}h.

(4.9)

Similarly, a bound for the term
T_{3}
can be derived as follows

\displaystyle T_{3}\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\gamma-1}\varrho_{k}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}\Big|\sum_{i=\eta(j,k)}^{r}e_{\tau}z_{i}(\tau)\nabla_{z}g(\tau,\mathbb{X}_{\tau})\Big|^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau\Big)^{2p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{j}\big|(\nabla X_{t_{j}})^{-1}\big|^{4p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}h.

Let us notice that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}\Big|\sum_{i=\eta(j,k)}^{r}e_{\tau}z_{i}(\tau)\nabla_{z}g(\tau,\mathbb{X}_{\tau})\Big|^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau\Big)^{2p}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\sup_{t_{j}\leq\tau\leq t_{j+1}}|\nabla_{z}g(\tau,\mathbb{X}_{\tau})|^{4p}\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}\Big|\sum_{i=\eta(j,k)}^{r}e_{\tau}z_{i}(\tau)\Big|^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau\Big)^{2p}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{p}\mathbb{E}\Big[1+\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}(\ell(|Y_{\tau}|)|{Z}_{\tau}|)^{8p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\times\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}\Big|\sum_{i=\eta(j,k)}^{r}e_{\tau}z_{i}(\tau)\Big|^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau\Big)^{4p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{p}(1+\|b\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T];C_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))})S(\varphi)^{4p},

where we used the growth
|\nabla_{z}g(\tau,\mathbb{X}_{\tau})|\leq\Lambda_{z}(1+\ell(|Y_{\tau}|)|Z_{\tau}|)
and the inequality (
4.8
), to derive the last two inequalities. Thus, there is a constant
C
(independent of
h
)such that the term
T_{3}
is controlled by

T_{3}\leq Ch.

(4.10)

Let us turn now to establish the estimate of the terms
T_{5},T_{6}
and
T_{7}
. Analogously to (
4.7
), we observe that

\displaystyle T_{5}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\gamma-1}\varrho_{k}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}\big|e_{\tau}z_{0}(\tau)\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau\Big)^{2p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{j}|(\nabla X_{t_{j}})^{-1}|^{4p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}h\leq Ch.

(4.11)

Indeed, using the reverse Hölder inequality once again and applying Lemma
3.9
lead to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}\big|e_{\tau}z_{0}(\tau)\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau\Big)^{2p}\Big]\leq

\displaystyle C(p_{1})\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}}\Big[\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}\big|e_{\tau}z_{0}(\tau)\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau\Big)^{2pp_{1}^{\prime}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{p_{1}^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(p_{1})\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}}\Big[\Big|e_{T}y_{0}(T)-y_{0}(0)\Big|^{4pp_{1}^{\prime}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{p_{1}^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(p_{1})\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}}\Big[\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}|e_{\tau}\nabla_{x}g(\tau,\mathbb{X}_{\tau})\nabla X_{\tau}|\mathrm{d}\tau\Big)^{4p_{1}p_{1}^{\prime}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{p_{1}^{\prime}}}.

By using the growth of
\nabla_{x}g
and the integrability of
\nabla X
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}\big|e_{\tau}z_{0}(\tau)\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau\Big)^{2p}\Big]\leq C(p_{1},p_{0},T,\|b\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T];C_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))},\|Z*W\|_{\operatorname{BMO}}).

(4.12)

The bounds of
T_{6}
and
T_{7}
follow similarly i.e. there exist a constant
C
independent of
h
such that

\displaystyle T_{6}+T_{7}\leq Ch.

(4.13)

Indeed, using similar estimates as above
T_{7}
for instance can be controlled by

\displaystyle T_{7}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\gamma-1}\varrho_{k}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\nabla_{{z}}g(\tau,\mathbb{X}_{\tau})\big|^{8p}+\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{t_{j+1}}}\Big|e_{\tau}z_{0}(\tau)\Big|^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau\Big)^{4p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\big|(\nabla X_{t_{j}})^{-1}\big|^{4p}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}h

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\sum_{k=0}^{\gamma-1}\varrho_{k}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\nabla_{{z}}g(\tau,\mathbb{X}_{\tau})\big|^{8p}+\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{t_{j+1}}}|e_{\tau}z_{0}(\tau)|^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau\Big)^{4p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}h

\displaystyle\leq Ch.

It remains to estimate
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
in order to conclude the proof. Let us start with
T_{2}
. Using the same technique as before again, we deduce that

\displaystyle T_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\gamma-1}\varrho_{k}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\Big|\sum_{i=l_{j}}^{\eta(j,k)-1}e_{t_{j}}y_{i}(t_{j})\Big|^{4p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{j}\big|(\nabla X_{t_{j}})^{-1}\big|^{4p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}h

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\gamma-1}\varrho_{k}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\Big|\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}}\Big[\sum_{i=l_{j}}^{\eta(j,k)-1}e_{T}y_{i}(T)\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}\Big]\Big|^{4p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{j}\big|(\nabla X_{t_{j}})^{-1}\big|^{4p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}h.

Recall that
\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{j}\big|(\nabla X_{t_{j}})^{-1}\big|^{4p}\Big]\leq C
, where the constant
C
does not depend on
h
(see (
2.6
)). Therefore by the definition of the terminal value

\displaystyle T_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(p_{1},p)\sum_{k=0}^{\gamma-1}\varrho_{k}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}}\Big[\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\Big|\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}}\Big[\sum_{i=l_{j}}^{\eta(j,k)-1}e_{T}y_{i}(T)\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}\Big]\Big|^{4pp_{1}^{\prime}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2p_{1}^{\prime}}}h

\displaystyle\leq C(p_{1},p)\sum_{k=0}^{\gamma-1}\varrho_{k}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\sum_{i=l_{j}}^{\eta(j,k)-1}S(\varphi)\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}}\big[A_{T}\sup_{0\leq s_{i}\leq T}|\nabla_{x}X_{s_{i}}|/\mathfrak{F}_{s}\big]\Big)^{4pp_{1}^{\prime}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2p_{1}^{\prime}}}h.

Using the Doob’s maximal inequality and the integrability of
\nabla_{x}X
we deduce that

\displaystyle T_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C(p_{1},p)(1+S(\varphi)^{2p})h.

(4.14)

Finally, for
\tau\in(t_{j},t_{j+1})
the term
T_{1}
can be rewritten as follow

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\sum_{k=l_{j}}^{l_{j+1}-1}\mathbb{E}\int_{s_{k}}^{s_{k+1}}\mathbb{E}\Big|h_{j}\nabla_{{y}}g(\tau,\mathbb{X}_{\tau})\sum_{i=k}^{r}e_{\tau}y_{i}(t)\Big|^{2p}\big|(\nabla X_{t_{j}})^{-1}\big|^{2p}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\sum_{k=l_{j}}^{l_{j+1}-1}\mathbb{E}\int_{s_{k}}^{s_{k+1}}\mathbb{E}\Big|h_{j}e_{\tau}\nabla_{{y}}g(\tau,\mathbb{X}_{\tau})\Big|^{4p}\big|(\nabla X_{t_{j}})^{-1}\big|^{2p}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\quad+C\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\sum_{k=l_{j}}^{l_{j+1}-1}\mathbb{E}\int_{s_{k}}^{s_{k+1}}\mathbb{E}\Big|\sum_{i=k}^{r}y_{i}(t_{j})\Big|^{4p}\big|(\nabla X_{t_{j}})^{-1}\big|^{2p}\mathrm{d}t,

where we used the elementary inequality
2ab\leq a^{2}+b^{2}
to obtain the inequality above. Therefore, by using once more the same technique we deduce that

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\sum_{k=0}^{\gamma-1}\varrho_{k}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}h_{j}\Big)^{8p}\big|e_{\tau}\nabla_{{y}}g(\tau,\mathbb{X}_{\tau})\big|^{8p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}h

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\sum_{k=l_{j}}^{l_{j+1}-1}\mathbb{E}\int_{s_{k}}^{s_{k+1}}\mathbb{E}\Big|\sum_{i=k}^{r}y_{i}(t_{j})\Big|^{8p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{j}\big|(\nabla X_{t_{j}})^{-1}\big|^{4p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\sum_{k=0}^{\gamma-1}\varrho_{k}T^{4p}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|e_{\tau}\nabla_{{y}}g(\tau,\mathbb{X}_{\tau})\big|^{8p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}h+C(p_{1})\sum_{k=0}^{\gamma-1}\varrho_{k}S(\varphi)^{4p}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}}\Big[\sup_{i}\big|\nabla X_{s_{i}}\big|^{8p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}h.

From the growth of
\nabla_{y}g
, the boundedness of
Y
, the integrability of
e_{\tau}
and the control from
T_{2}
we have that

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}T^{4p}\mathbb{E}\Big[(1+\ell(|Y_{\tau}|)\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}|Z_{\tau}|^{2})^{16p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{4}}h+C(p_{1},p_{0})(1+\|b\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T];C_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))})S(\varphi)^{4p}h

\displaystyle\leq C(p,T)(1+S(\varphi)^{8p})h+C(p_{1},p_{0})(1+\|b\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T];C_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))})S(\varphi)^{4p}h.

(4.15)

In summary, combining (
4.2
), (
4.9
), (
4.10
), (
4.11
), (
4.13
), (
4.14
) and (
4.15
), there is a constant
C
not depending on
h
and the Lipschitz constants of the coefficients such that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|Z_{t}-Z_{t_{j}}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\Big)^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}h^{p-1}\Big[\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}h_{j}^{2p}\mathrm{d}t+\sum_{\nu=1}^{7}T_{\nu}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}(1+\|b\|_{L^{\infty}([0,T];C_{b}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))})h^{p}.

By using standard smooth approximation techniques in combination with result on stability for quadratic BSDEs (see for instance
[
21
, Corollary 3.9]
), the above estimate remains valid for non continuously differentiable coefficients. This concludes the proof.
∎

Let us briefly provide below some arguments for the proof of Theorem
1.4
.

Proof of Theorem
1.4
.

In this context, clearly the path regularity of the process
Y
follows similarly as it was done in the case of the discrete-type functional terminal value, since the control
Z
satisfies the bound (
3.25
). Thus

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{s\leq r\leq t}|Y_{r}-Y_{s}|^{p}\Big]\leq C(p)(1+\Lambda_{\Phi}^{p})\Big\{|t-s|^{p}+|t-s|^{p/2}\Big\}.

We focus on the control process part. Let us consider the following sequence of partitions
(\pi(r))_{r\geq 1}:0=s_{0}<s_{1}\cdots<s_{r}=T
of the interval
[0,T]
such that
\lim_{r\rightarrow\infty}|\pi(r)|=0
. We define accordingly the smooth bounded function
\hat{\varphi}^{\pi(r)}(X_{s_{0}},\cdots,X_{s_{r}})
approximating the functional
\Phi
(see Remark
3.3
). In addition, we impose that the Lipschitz constant of
\hat{\varphi}^{\pi(r)}
satisfies
S(\hat{\varphi}^{\pi(r)})<C\Lambda_{\Phi}
. If we denote by
(Y^{\pi(r)},Z^{\pi(r)})
the solution corresponding to the terminal value
\hat{\varphi}^{\pi(r)}(X_{s_{0}},\cdots,X_{s_{r}})
, then invoking once more a stability result for quadratic BSDEs (see
[
21
, Corollary 3.9]
), it is readily seen that

\lim_{|\pi(r)|\rightarrow 0}\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}|Z^{\pi(r)}_{t}-Z_{t}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t=0.

Then, there is a subsequence still indexed by
{\pi(r)}
such that
\lim_{|\pi(r)|\rightarrow 0}\mathbb{E}|Z^{\pi(r)}_{t}-Z_{t}|^{2}=0

\mathrm{d}t\text{-a.e.}
Moreover,
one can extract another subsequence that we will still denote by
Z^{\pi(r)}
such that
\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}

\text{Leb}\{t\in[0,T),Z_{t}^{\pi(r)}=Z_{t}\}
goes to
T
as
|\pi(r)|
tends to
0
.
In addition, for all
p>p_{0}^{\prime}
there is a constant
C>0
not depending on the particular choice of the partition such that
\mathbb{E}|Z_{t}^{\pi(r)}|^{2p}\leq C
, for all
t\in[0,T]
. This leads to the fact
G^{\pi(r)}(t):=\mathbb{E}|Z_{t}^{\pi(r)}-Z_{t}|^{2p}
goes to
0
as
|\pi(r)|\rightarrow 0
,
\mathrm{d}t\text{ a.e.}
by the dominated convergence theorem. Thus
G^{\pi(r)}(t)
is bounded
\mathrm{d}t\text{ a.e.}
and uniformly in
\pi(r)
. Let us now establish the path regularity result for the limit process
Z_{t}
. We observe for all
t\in[t_{j},t_{j+1}]
that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|Z_{t}-Z_{t_{j}}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\Big)^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}\mathbb{E}(|Z_{t}^{\pi(r)}-Z_{t}|^{2p}+|Z_{t}^{\pi(r)}-Z_{t_{j}}^{\pi(r)}|^{2p}+|Z_{t_{j}}^{\pi(r)}-Z_{t_{j}}|^{2p})\mathrm{d}t

(4.16)

By using once more the dominated convergence theorem, we observe that the first and the last terms on the right hand side from the above inequality tends to 0 as
\pi(r)\rightarrow 0
. Therefore, there is a constant
C
not depending on
\pi(r)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|Z_{t}-Z_{t_{j}}|^{2}\mathrm{d}t\Big)^{p}\leq C(p)\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}\mathbb{E}|Z_{t}^{\pi(r)}-Z_{t_{j}}^{\pi(r)}|^{2p}\mathrm{d}t\leq Ch^{p}.

(4.17)

This finishes the proof.
∎

Proof of Proposition
1.6
.

We mimic the proof of
[
54
, Proposition 3.2.12]
. We will first assume that the terminal value is given as in the proof of Theorem
1.5
. Furthermore, we assume that the coefficients involved are continuously differentiable with bounded derivatives. We underline the fact that, thanks to the assumptions in force, the trade between the underlying probability
\mathbb{P}
and its equivalent measure
\mathbb{Q}^{N}
is possible at a cost of the constant
\epsilon\in(0,1)
.

We first observe that

\displaystyle|Z_{s}-\hat{Z}_{t_{j}}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2|Z_{s}-{Z}_{t_{j}}|^{2}+2\left|Z_{t_{j}}-\frac{1}{\delta t_{j}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}Z_{s}\mathrm{d}s\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}\Big]\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2|Z_{s}-{Z}_{t_{j}}|^{2}+\frac{2}{\epsilon\delta t_{j}}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{N}}\Big[\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|Z_{t_{j}}-Z_{s}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}\Big]

Integrating from
t_{j}
to
t_{j+1}
and taking the
\eta^{th}\text{power}
, for any
\eta>1
we have

\displaystyle\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|Z_{s}-\hat{Z}_{t_{j}}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{\eta}\leq\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|Z_{s}-{Z}_{t_{j}}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{\eta}+\Big(\frac{2}{\epsilon}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{N}}\Big[\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|Z_{t_{j}}-Z_{s}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}\Big]\Big)^{\eta}.

For any
p\geq 2
, and using Jensen’s inequality, there is a constant
C(\epsilon,p,\eta)>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N-1}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{N}}\Big\{\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|Z_{s}-\hat{Z}_{t_{j}}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{\eta}\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big\}^{p}\Big]\leq C(\epsilon,p,\eta)h^{p(\eta-1)}I,

where

\displaystyle I=\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N-1}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{N}}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|Z_{s}-{Z}_{t_{j}}|^{2\eta}\mathrm{d}s\Big)\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]^{p}\Big].

Let us assume without loss of generality that
\pi_{1}
is finer than
\pi_{0}
and that
t_{i}=s_{l_{i}}
for some increasing sequence
(l_{i})_{i=1}^{N}
. For any
t\in[s_{k},s_{k+1})\subset[s_{l_{j}},s_{l_{j+1}})=[t_{j},t_{j+1})
, we write

\displaystyle Z_{s}-Z_{t_{j}}

\displaystyle=\left(\nabla^{k}Y_{s}\right)^{\mathbb{T}}\left(\nabla_{x}X_{s}\right)^{-1}-\left(\nabla^{l_{j}}Y_{t_{j}}\right)^{\mathbb{T}}\left(\nabla_{x}X_{t_{j}}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=I_{s}^{1,j}+I_{s}^{2,j},

where
I_{s}^{1,j}:=\left(\nabla^{l_{j}}Y_{s}\right)^{\mathbb{T}}\left((\nabla_{x}X_{s})^{-1}-(\nabla_{x}X_{t_{j}})^{-1}\right)
and
I_{s}^{2,j}:=\left(\nabla^{k}Y_{s}-\nabla^{l_{j}}Y_{t_{j}}\right)^{\mathbb{T}}\left(\nabla_{x}X_{t_{j}}\right)^{-1}.
Therefore, from Girsanov’s theorem and by applying the reverse Hölder inequality we deduce that

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle\leq C_{pq^{*}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|I_{s}^{1,j}|^{2\eta}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{pq^{*}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{q^{*}}}+\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N-1}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{N}}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|I_{s}^{2,j}|^{2\eta}\mathrm{d}s\Big)\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]^{p}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C_{pq^{*}}\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\mathbb{E}(|I_{s}^{1,j}|^{2pq^{*}\eta}){\bf{1}_{[t_{j},t_{j+1})}}\mathrm{d}s\Big)+\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N-1}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{N}}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|I_{s}^{2,j}|^{2\eta}\mathrm{d}s\Big)\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]^{p}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C_{pq^{*}}h^{pq^{*}\eta}+\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N-1}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{N}}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|I_{s}^{2,j}|^{2\eta}\mathrm{d}s\Big)\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]^{p}\Big],

where one can use similar computation as in (
4.2
) to obtain the first bound of the inequality above and the constant
C_{pq^{*}}
does not depend on the Lipschitz constant of
b
.

On the other hand, we will follow the same strategy we used to estimate for instance (
4.5
), by considering the same finer partition
\pi_{2}
. We first observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{N}}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|I_{s}^{2,j}|^{2\eta}\mathrm{d}s\Big)\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq(2-\epsilon)\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{N}}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|I_{s}^{2,j}|^{2\eta}\mathrm{d}s\Big)\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}\Big]\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq(2-\epsilon)\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{N}}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\mathbb{E}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}\big[\big|\nabla^{k}Y_{s}-\nabla^{l_{j}}Y_{t_{j}}\big|^{2\eta}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}\big]|(\nabla X_{t_{j}})^{-1}|^{2\eta}\mathrm{d}s\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big].

By using the expression given by (
4.5
) and applying once more the Girsanov theorem, we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{N}}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|I_{s}^{2,j}|^{2\eta}\mathrm{d}s\Big)\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\eta}(2-\epsilon)\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{N}}\Bigg[\sum_{k=0}^{\gamma-1}\varrho_{k}\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\frac{1}{\epsilon}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{N}}\Big[\Big|(t_{j+1}-t_{j})\nabla_{y}g(\tau,\mathbb{X}_{\tau})\sum_{i=k}^{r}e_{\tau}y_{i}(t_{j})\Big|^{2\eta}

\displaystyle+\Big|\sum_{i\geq l_{j}}^{k-1}e_{t_{j}}y_{i}(t_{j})\Big|^{2\eta}+\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t}\sum_{i\geq k}^{r}e_{\tau}\nabla_{{z}}g({\tau},\mathbb{X}_{\tau})z_{i}({\tau})\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{2\eta}+\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}\Big|\sum_{i\geq k}^{r}e_{\tau}z_{i}({\tau})\Big|^{2}\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{\eta}

\displaystyle+\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}\big|e_{\tau}z_{0}({\tau})\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{\eta}+\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}|e_{\tau}\nabla_{{x}}g({\tau},\mathbb{X}_{\tau})\nabla_{x}{X}_{\tau}|\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{2\eta}

\displaystyle\quad+\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}|e_{\tau}\nabla_{{z}}g({\tau},\mathbb{X}_{\tau})z_{0}({\tau})|\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{2\eta}\Big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}|(\nabla X_{t_{j}})^{-1}|^{2\eta}\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]\Bigg]h.

By using the Hölder’s inequality, we deduce the following for any
p\geq q^{*}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N-1}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{N}}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|I_{s}^{2,j}|^{2\eta}\mathrm{d}s\Big)\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]^{p}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C({\eta},p,\epsilon)\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N-1}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{N}}\Bigg[\sum_{k=0}^{\gamma-1}\varrho_{k}\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\frac{1}{\epsilon}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{N}}\Bigg(\Big|(t_{j+1}-t_{j})\nabla_{y}g(\tau,\mathbb{X}_{\tau})\sum_{i=k}^{r}e_{\tau}y_{i}(t)\Big|^{2\eta}

\displaystyle+\Big|\sum_{i\geq l_{j}}^{k-1}e_{t_{j}}y_{i}(t_{j})\Big|^{2\eta}+\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t}\sum_{i\geq k}^{r}e_{\tau}\nabla_{{z}}g({\tau},\mathbb{X}_{\tau})z_{i}({\tau})\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{2\eta}+\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}\Big|\sum_{i\geq k}^{r}e_{\tau}z_{i}({\tau})\Big|^{2}\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{\eta}

\displaystyle+\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}\big|e_{\tau}z_{0}({\tau})\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{\eta}+\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}|e_{\tau}\nabla_{{x}}g({\tau},\mathbb{X}_{\tau})\nabla_{x}{X}_{\tau}|\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{2\eta}

\displaystyle\quad+\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}|e_{\tau}\nabla_{{z}}g({\tau},\mathbb{X}_{\tau})z_{0}({\tau})|\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{2\eta}\Big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}\Bigg)|(\nabla X_{t_{j}})^{-1}|^{2\eta}\Big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Bigg]^{p}\Bigg]h^{p}.

Moreover, by using the tower property and the reverse Hölder inequality we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N-1}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{N}}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|I_{s}^{2,j}|^{2\eta}\mathrm{d}s\Big)\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]^{p}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C({\eta},p,\epsilon)\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N-1}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\gamma-1}\varrho_{k}\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\Bigg\{\Big|(t_{j+1}-t_{j})\nabla_{y}g(\tau,\mathbb{X}_{\tau})\sum_{i=k}^{r}e_{\tau}y_{i}(t)\Big|^{2\eta}

\displaystyle+\Big|\sum_{i\geq l_{j}}^{k-1}e_{t_{j}}y_{i}(t_{j})\Big|^{2\eta}+\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t}\sum_{i\geq k}^{r}e_{\tau}\nabla_{{z}}g({\tau},\mathbb{X}_{\tau})z_{i}({\tau})\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{2\eta}+\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}\Big|\sum_{i\geq k}^{r}e_{\tau}z_{i}({\tau})\Big|^{2}\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{\eta}

\displaystyle+\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}\big|e_{\tau}z_{0}({\tau})\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{\eta}+\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}|e_{\tau}\nabla_{{x}}g({\tau},\mathbb{X}_{\tau})\nabla_{x}{X}_{\tau}|\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{2\eta}

\displaystyle\quad+\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}|e_{\tau}\nabla_{{z}}g({\tau},\mathbb{X}_{\tau})z_{0}({\tau})|\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{2\eta}\Bigg\}\Bigg)^{q^{*}}\sup_{0\leq j\leq N-1}|(\nabla X_{t_{j}})^{-1}|^{2\eta q^{*}}\Big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Bigg]^{\frac{p}{q^{*}}}\Bigg]h^{p}.

This leads to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N-1}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{N}}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|I_{s}^{2,j}|^{2\eta}\mathrm{d}s\Big)\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]^{p}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C({\eta},p,\epsilon,q^{*})\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{\gamma-1}\varrho_{k}\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\Bigg\{\Big|(t_{j+1}-t_{j})\nabla_{y}g(\tau,\mathbb{X}_{\tau})\sum_{i=k}^{r}e_{\tau}y_{i}(t)\Big|^{2\eta}

\displaystyle+\Big|\sum_{i\geq l_{j}}^{k-1}e_{t_{j}}y_{i}(t_{j})\Big|^{2\eta}+\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t}\sum_{i\geq k}^{r}e_{\tau}\nabla_{{z}}g({\tau},\mathbb{X}_{\tau})z_{i}({\tau})\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{2\eta}+\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}\Big|\sum_{i\geq k}^{r}e_{\tau}z_{i}({\tau})\Big|^{2}\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{\eta}

\displaystyle+\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}\big|e_{\tau}z_{0}({\tau})\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{\eta}+\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}|e_{\tau}\nabla_{{x}}g({\tau},\mathbb{X}_{\tau})\nabla_{x}{X}_{\tau}|\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{2\eta}

\displaystyle\quad+\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}|e_{\tau}\nabla_{{z}}g({\tau},\mathbb{X}_{\tau})z_{0}({\tau})|\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{2\eta}\Bigg\}\Bigg)^{p}\sup_{0\leq j\leq N-1}|(\nabla X_{t_{j}})^{-1}|^{2\eta p}\Bigg]h^{p}.

Hence, using once more the Hölder inequality we obtain the existence of the constant
C({\eta},p,\epsilon,q^{*})
not depending on the Lipschitz constants of the coefficients such that the following holds

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N-1}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{N}}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|I_{s}^{2,j}|^{2\eta}\mathrm{d}s\Big)\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]^{p}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C({\eta},p,\epsilon,q^{*})h^{p}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{k=0}^{\gamma-1}\varrho_{k}\Bigg(\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\Bigg\{\Big|(t_{j+1}-t_{j})\nabla_{y}g(\tau,\mathbb{X}_{\tau})\sum_{i=k}^{r}e_{\tau}y_{i}(t)\Big|^{2\eta}

\displaystyle+\Big|\sum_{i\geq l_{j}}^{k-1}e_{t_{j}}y_{i}(t_{j})\Big|^{2\eta}+\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t}\sum_{i\geq k}^{r}e_{\tau}\nabla_{{z}}g({\tau},\mathbb{X}_{\tau})z_{i}({\tau})\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{2\eta}+\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}\Big|\sum_{i\geq k}^{r}e_{\tau}z_{i}({\tau})\Big|^{2}\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{\eta}

\displaystyle+\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}\big|e_{\tau}z_{0}({\tau})\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{\eta}+\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}|e_{\tau}\nabla_{{x}}g({\tau},\mathbb{X}_{\tau})\nabla_{x}{X}_{\tau}|\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{2\eta}

\displaystyle\quad+\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}|e_{\tau}\nabla_{{z}}g({\tau},\mathbb{X}_{\tau})z_{0}({\tau})|\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{2\eta}\Bigg\}\Bigg)^{2p}\Bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

By rearranging the terms above, we observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N-1}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{N}}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|I_{s}^{2,j}|^{2\eta}\mathrm{d}s\Big)\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]^{p}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C({\eta},p,\epsilon,q^{*})h^{p}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{k=0}^{\gamma-1}\varrho_{k}\Bigg(\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\Bigg\{\Big|(t_{j+1}-t_{j})\nabla_{y}g(\tau,\mathbb{X}_{\tau})\sum_{i=k}^{r}e_{\tau}y_{i}(t)\Big|^{2\eta}

\displaystyle+\Big|\sum_{i\geq l_{j}}^{k-1}e_{t_{j}}y_{i}(t_{j})\Big|^{2\eta}+\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t}\sum_{i\geq k}^{r}e_{\tau}\nabla_{{z}}g({\tau},\mathbb{X}_{\tau})z_{i}({\tau})\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{2\eta}+\Big(\int_{{t_{j}}}^{t_{j+1}}\Big|\sum_{i\geq k}^{r}e_{\tau}z_{i}({\tau})\Big|^{2}\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Big)^{\eta}\Bigg\}\Bigg)^{2p}\Bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+C({\eta},p,\epsilon,q^{*})h^{p}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\Bigg(\int_{{t_{i}}}^{T}\big|e_{\tau}z_{0}({\tau})\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Bigg)^{2p\eta}+\Bigg(\int_{{t_{i}}}^{T}|e_{\tau}\nabla_{{x}}g({\tau},\mathbb{X}_{\tau})\nabla_{x}{X}_{\tau}|\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Bigg)^{4p\eta}

\displaystyle\quad+\Bigg(\int_{{t_{i}}}^{T}|e_{\tau}\nabla_{{z}}g({\tau},\mathbb{X}_{\tau})z_{0}({\tau})|\mathrm{d}{\tau}\Bigg)^{4p\eta}\Bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

By using the same scheme as in the proof of Theorem
1.5
, all the terms from the right hand side of the above inequality can be approximated similarly. Hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N-1}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{N}}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|I_{s}^{2,j}|^{2\eta}\mathrm{d}s\Big)\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]^{p}\Big]\leq

\displaystyle C({\eta},p,\epsilon,q^{*})(h^{p\eta}+h^{p})

Therefore, there is a constant
C>0
not depending on
h
and on the Lipschitz constants of the coefficients such that for any
p>q^{*}
and
\eta>1
the following holds

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N-1}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{N}}\Big\{\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|Z_{s}-\hat{Z}_{t_{j}}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{\eta}\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big\}^{p}\Big]\leq Ch^{p(\eta-1)}(h^{p\eta}+h^{p})\leq Ch^{p\eta}.

Let us turn now to the general case. By following the same density argument as developed in the proof of Theorem
1.4
, we recall that
\lim_{|\pi(r)|\rightarrow 0}Z_{t}^{\pi(r)}=Z_{t}

\mathrm{d}t\otimes\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}
It is then enough to control the terms below to derive the sought estimate. Using the reverse Hölder inequality for all
p>q^{*}
, there is a constant
C>0
not depending on
\pi(r)
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N-1}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{N}}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|Z_{t}^{\pi(r)}-Z_{t}|^{2\eta}+|Z_{t_{j}}^{\pi(r)}-Z_{t_{j}}|^{2\eta}\mathrm{d}s\Big)\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]^{p}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|Z_{t}^{\pi(r)}-Z_{t}|^{2\eta}+|Z_{t_{j}}^{\pi(r)}-Z_{t_{j}}|^{2\eta}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{q^{*}}\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]^{p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{q^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq Ch^{pq^{*}-1}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\mathbb{E}[|Z_{t}^{\pi(r)}-Z_{t}|^{2\eta pq^{*}}+|Z_{t_{j}}^{\pi(r)}-Z_{t_{j}}|^{2\eta pq^{*}}]{\bf{1}}_{[t_{j},t_{j+1})}\mathrm{d}s\right]^{\frac{1}{q^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq Ch^{pq^{*}-1}\left[\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\mathbb{E}(|Z_{t}^{\pi(r)}-Z_{t}|^{2\eta pq^{*}}+|Z_{t_{j}}^{\pi(r)}-Z_{t_{j}}|^{2\eta pq^{*}}){\bf{1}}_{[t_{j},t_{j+1})}\mathrm{d}s\right]^{\frac{1}{q^{*}}},

the latter tends to
0
when
\pi(r)\rightarrow 0
by using the dominated convergence theorem.
∎

Proof of Corollary
1.11
.

Let us first notice that the Euler scheme (
1.14
) can be rewritten as follows on the partition
\pi:0=s_{0}<s_{1}<\cdots<s_{r}=1

\displaystyle\mathscr{X}^{\pi}(t)

\displaystyle=x+\int_{0}^{t}b(s,\mathscr{X}_{\pi(s)}^{\pi})\mathrm{d}s+W_{t}

\displaystyle\mathscr{X}^{\pi}_{t}

\displaystyle:=\mathscr{X}^{\pi}({\pi(t)})

\displaystyle\pi(s)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{i=0}^{r-1}t_{i}{\bf 1}_{[t_{i},t_{i+1})}(s)+{\bf 1}_{T}(s)

From Theorem, the following holds for all
p\geq 1

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\sup_{0\leq t\leq 1}|X_{t}-\mathscr{X}^{\pi}(t)|^{2p}\big]\leq Ch^{(1-\gamma)p}

(4.18)

On the other hand, for every
p\geq 1
we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\sup_{0\leq t\leq 1}|X_{t}-\mathscr{X}_{t}^{\pi}|^{2p}]

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\mathbb{E}[\sup_{0\leq t\leq 1}|X_{t}-\mathscr{X}^{\pi}(t)|^{2p}]+C(p)\mathbb{E}[\max_{0\leq i\leq r-1}\sup_{t_{i}\leq t\leq t_{i+1}}|\mathscr{X}_{t_{i}}^{\pi}-\mathscr{X}_{t}^{\pi}|^{2p}]

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\mathbb{E}[\sup_{0\leq t\leq 1}|X_{t}-\mathscr{X}^{\pi}(t)|^{2p}]+C(p)h^{2p}+C(p)\mathbb{E}[\max_{0\leq i\leq r-1}\sup_{t_{i}\leq t\leq t_{i+1}}|W_{t}-W_{t_{i}}|^{4p}]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(p)\Big[h^{(1-\gamma)p}+\Big(h\log\big(\frac{1}{h}\big)\Big)^{p}\Big].

∎

4.2.
Proof of Theorem
1.7

As quoted earlier, Theorem
1.7
is a consequence of Lemma
1.8
. Therefore, in this subsection, we prove Lemma
1.8
.

Proof of Lemma
1.8
.

We will denote by
\Lambda>0
the bound of
H
. Let
(H_{n})\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{K}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
be a sequence approximating
H
such that
\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}|H_{n}|\leq\Lambda
. Set

G_{n}(\mathbb{y};x):=\mathscr{X}_{F}(\mathbb{y})H_{n}(\mathbb{y};x),\quad G(\mathbb{y};x):=\mathscr{X}_{F}(\mathbb{y})H(\mathbb{y};x)

where
F\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
is a Borel set such that
F^{c}
is of Lebesgue measure null. We observe that
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}G_{n}(\mathbb{y};\cdot)=G(\mathbb{y};\cdot),\text{Leb}_{d}\text{-a.e.,}
for all
\mathbb{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{K}
. Similarly, we also define
U^{n}
with
G_{n}
in lieu of
G
. Hence, the couple

(Y_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x},Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x})=\left(U^{n}(\mathbb{y};s,X_{s}^{r,x}),\nabla_{x}U^{n}(\mathbb{y};s,X_{s}^{r,x})\right)_{s\in[t,R]}

solves the following BSDE of interest on the interval
[t,R]

\displaystyle Y_{t}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}=G_{n}(\mathbb{y};X_{R}^{r,x})+\int_{t}^{R}g(s,X_{s}^{r,x},Y_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x},Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x})\mathrm{d}s-\int_{t}^{R}Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}\mathrm{d}W_{s}.

(4.19)

Moreover, since
G_{n}
is bounded and
g
is continuous and of quadratic growth in the control variable, for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the BSDE (
4.19
) has a unique solution
\left(U^{n}(\mathbb{y};s,X_{s}^{r,x}),\nabla_{x}U^{n}(\mathbb{y};s,X_{s}^{r,x})\right)_{s\in[t,R]}\in\mathscr{S}^{\infty}\times\mathscr{H}_{BMO}
(see for e.g.,
[
2
]
).

Uniform bound of
U^{n}(y;t,x)
:

If
U^{n}_{0}(y;t,x)
stands for the solution to (
4.19
) with null driver, then from
[
21
, Lemma 3.8]
we deduce that

\displaystyle|U^{n}(\mathbb{y};t,x)-U^{n}_{0}(\mathbb{y};t,x)|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\int_{t}^{R}|g(s,X_{s}^{t,x},Y_{s,0}^{\mathbb{y},n;t,x},Z_{s,0}^{\mathbb{y},n;t,x})|\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2p}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\int_{t}^{R}1+|\mathbb{E}(G_{n}(\mathbb{y};X_{R}^{t,x})/\mathfrak{F}_{s})|+|Z_{s,0}^{\mathbb{y},n;t,x}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2p}}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\Lambda)R+\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\int_{t}^{R}|Z_{s,0}^{\mathbb{y},n;t,x}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2p}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2p}}.

On the other hand,
Z_{s,0}^{\mathbb{y},n;t,x}
represents the control variable of the solution to our BSDE of interest when the driver is null. Standard computations lead to the following estimate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{t}^{R}|Z_{s,0}^{\mathbb{y},n;t,x}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2p}\leq C(p)\mathbb{E}(G_{n}(\mathbb{y};X_{R}^{t,x}))^{2p}\leq C(p,q)\Lambda^{2p}.

Therefore, there exists a constant
C>0
(independent of
n
) such that

\displaystyle|U^{n}(\mathbb{y};t,x)|\leq C.

(4.20)

Uniform BMO bound of
Z^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}
:

Let us first recall the following refinement concerning Lyapunov functions (see
[
52
]
).

Definition 4.1

(Lyapunov function)
.

Let
g:[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be a Borel function and
\mathbb{c}>0
be a constant. A pair
(h,k)
of non negative functions with
h\in W^{1,2}_{loc}(\mathbb{R})
and
k
a Borel function is said to be a
\mathbb{c}\text{-Lyapunov}
pair for
g
if
h(0)=0,

h^{\prime}(0)=0
and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime\prime}(y)|z|^{2}-h^{\prime}(y)g(t,x,y,z)\geq|z|^{2}-k,

(4.21)

for all
(t,x,y,z)\in[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R},
with
|y|\leq\mathbb{c}.
We will denote
(h,k)\in\text{\bf Ly}(g,\mathbb{c})
.

In contrast to
[
52
, Definition 2.3]
, the definition requires the function
h
to be only differentiable in the weak sense. This approach suits to our context, as the function
\ell
in Assumption
1.1
is not assumed to be continuous.

Lemma 4.2
.

Assume that
g
satisfies the growth
(iii)
. Then, there is a Lyapunov pair
(h,k)
depending on such that
(h,k)\in\text{\bf Ly}(g,\mathbb{c})
.

Proof.

Let us choose
h(y)=h_{1}(y)-y
, where

h_{1}(y)=\int_{0}^{y}\exp\Big(\kappa\int_{0}^{w}\ell(u)\mathrm{d}u\Big)\mathrm{d}w,

where
\kappa>0
is a free constant.
We notice that a.e.
h^{\prime}(y)=\exp\left(\kappa\int_{0}^{y}\ell(u)\mathrm{d}u\right)-1
and

h^{\prime\prime}(y)=\kappa\ell(y)\exp\left(\kappa\int_{0}^{y}\ell(u)\mathrm{d}u\right)
and clearly satisfy
h(0)=h^{\prime}(0)=0.
Then, from the growth of
g
we deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime\prime}(y)|z|^{2}-h^{\prime}(y)g(t,x,y,z)

\displaystyle=\frac{\kappa}{2}\ell(y)|z|^{2}\exp\Big(\kappa\int_{0}^{y}\ell(u)\mathrm{d}u\Big)-\Big[\exp\Big(\kappa\int_{0}^{y}\ell(u)\mathrm{d}u\Big)-1\Big]g(t,x,y,z)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\kappa}{2}\ell(y)|z|^{2}\exp\Big(\kappa\int_{0}^{y}\ell(u)\mathrm{d}u\Big)-\Lambda(1+|y|+\ell(y)|z|^{2})\Big[\exp\Big(\kappa\int_{0}^{y}\ell(u)\mathrm{d}u\Big)-1\Big]

\displaystyle\geq\Lambda\ell(y)|z|^{2}-\Lambda e^{\kappa\|\ell\|_{L^{1}[0,\mathbb{c}]}}(1+\mathbb{c}),

the last inequality follows from the fact that the function
\ell
is non negative and we choose
\kappa=4\Lambda
. We conclude the proof by taking
\Lambda=\left(\|\ell\|_{L^{1}[0,\mathbb{c}]}\right)^{-1}
.
∎

Then, from the Itô-Krylov formula for BSDEs (see
[
3
]
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}h(Y_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x})

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime\prime}(Y_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x})|Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}|^{2}-h^{\prime}(Y_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x})g(s,X_{s}^{r,x},Y_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x},Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x})\Big)\mathrm{d}s+\mathrm{d}M_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x},

(4.22)

where
M_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}
is a martingale. For any stopping time
\tau\in[t,R]
and applying the property of the Lyapunov pair
(h,k)
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{\tau}^{R}|Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;t,x}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s/\mathfrak{F}_{\tau}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[h(G_{n}(\mathbb{y};X_{R}^{t,x}))-h(U^{n}(\mathbb{y};\tau,X_{\tau}^{t,x}))+k(R-\tau)/\mathfrak{F}_{\tau}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq 2\|h\circ U^{n}\|_{L^{\infty}}+kR.

Uniform bound of
\nabla_{x}U^{n}(\mathbb{y};t,x)
:

Let us now establish a bound for the gradient of the solution
U^{n}(\mathbb{y};r,x)
, uniformly in
n
. We assume
H\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{K\times d},\mathbb{R})
and that the driver
g
is continuously differentiable, while the drift
b
is uniformly Lipschitz continuous.
We will consider the following FBSDE for all
0\leq r\leq t\leq R

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathrm{d}X_{t}^{r,x}=\mathrm{d}\tilde{W}_{t},\\
\mathrm{d}Y_{t}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}=-(Z_{t}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}b(t,X_{t}^{r,x})+g(t,X_{t}^{r,x},Y_{t}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x},Z_{t}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}))\mathrm{d}t+Z_{t}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}\mathrm{d}\tilde{W}_{t},\\
X_{r}^{r,x}=x,Y_{R}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}=G_{n}(\mathbb{y};X_{R}^{r,x}),\end{cases}

(4.23)

where
\tilde{W}_{t}=W_{t}+\int_{0}^{t}b(v,X_{v}^{r,x})\mathrm{d}v
is a Brownian motion under the probability measure
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
given by
\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathbb{P}}/\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}:=\mathscr{E}(\int_{0}^{t}b(v,X_{v}^{r,x})\mathrm{d}W_{v})
, and
(Y_{\cdot}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x},Z_{\cdot}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x})=(U^{n}(\mathbb{y};\cdot,X_{\cdot}^{r,x}),\nabla_{x}U^{n}(\mathbb{y};\cdot,X_{\cdot}^{r,x}))
.

Under the above setting, we know that the FBSDE of interest is differentiable with respect to the initial value
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
under the probability measure
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
. Moreover, the dynamics of the derivative process is given by

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}Y_{t}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}

\displaystyle=\nabla_{x}G_{n}(\mathbb{y};X_{R}^{r,x})\nabla_{x}X_{R}^{r,x}+\int_{t}^{R}Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}\nabla_{x}b(s,X_{s}^{r,x})\nabla_{x}X_{s}^{r,x}\mathrm{d}s-\int_{t}^{R}\nabla_{x}Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}\mathrm{d}\tilde{W}_{s}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{t}^{R}b(s,X_{r}^{r,x})\nabla_{x}Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{t}^{R}\langle\nabla g(s,\mathbb{X}_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}),\nabla\mathbb{X}_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}\rangle\mathrm{d}s,

where
\mathbb{X}_{s}^{y,n;r,x}=({X}_{s}^{r,x},{Y}_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x},{Z}_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x})
. Or, equivalently under the measure
\mathrm{d}\mathbb{Q}/\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathrm{P}}=\mathscr{E}(\int_{0}^{\cdot}b(s,X^{r,x}_{s})+\nabla_{z}g(s,\mathbb{X}_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x})\mathrm{d}\tilde{W}_{s})
, it writes

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}Y_{t}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}

\displaystyle=e^{\int_{t}^{R}\nabla_{y}g(s,\mathbb{X}_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x})\mathrm{d}s}\nabla_{x}G_{n}(\mathbb{y};X_{R}^{r,x})\nabla_{x}X_{R}^{r,x}-\int_{t}^{R}e^{\int_{t}^{s}\nabla_{y}g(v,\mathbb{X}_{v}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x})\mathrm{d}v}\nabla_{x}Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}\mathrm{d}\mathbb{B}_{s}

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{t}^{R}e^{\int_{t}^{s}\nabla_{y}g(v,\mathbb{X}_{v}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x})\mathrm{d}v}Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}\nabla_{x}b(s,X_{s}^{r,x})\nabla_{x}X_{s}^{r,x}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{t}^{R}e^{\int_{t}^{s}\nabla_{y}g(v,\mathbb{X}_{v}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x})\mathrm{d}v}\nabla_{x}g(s,X_{s}^{r,x},Y_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x},Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x})\nabla_{x}{X}_{s}^{r,x}\mathrm{d}s,

with
\mathbb{B}_{t}:=\tilde{W}_{t}-\int_{0}^{t}b(s,X^{r,x}_{s})+\nabla_{z}g(s,\mathbb{X}_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x})\mathrm{d}s
, which is a Brownian motion under
\mathbb{Q}
. It is important to note that the dynamics of the first variation process
\nabla_{x}X_{t}^{r,x}
remains unchanged under the new probability measure
\mathbb{Q}
. Hence, by taking the conditional expectation, we obtain that

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}Y_{t}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}}\Big[e^{\int_{t}^{R}\nabla_{y}g(s,\mathbb{X}_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x})\mathrm{d}s}\nabla_{x}G_{n}(\mathbb{y};X_{R}^{r,x})+\int_{t}^{R}e^{\int_{t}^{s}\nabla_{y}g(v,\mathbb{X}_{v}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x})\mathrm{d}v}Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}\nabla_{x}b(s,X_{s}^{r,x})\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\int_{t}^{R}e^{\int_{t}^{s}\nabla_{y}g(v,\mathbb{X}_{v}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x})\mathrm{d}v}\nabla_{x}g(s,X_{s}^{r,x},Y_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x},Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x})\mathrm{d}s\Big/\mathfrak{F}_{t}\Big].

(4.24)

In the case where
H
is Lipschitz continuous and the driver
g
is also Lipschitz continuous in
x
and
y
, we obtain the following uniform bound of
Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}|Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}|\leq C\quad\text{for all }\mathbb{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{K},

where
C
is a constant depending on
\|\nabla_{x}b\|_{\infty},\|\nabla_{x}G_{n}\|_{\infty}
,
\|Z^{\mathbb{y},n,r,x}*W\|_{BMO}
and the Lipschitz constants of
g
in
x
and
y
respectively.

Let us recall that
\nabla_{x}Y_{t}^{\mathbb{y}.n;r,x}(\nabla_{x}X_{t}^{r,x})^{-1}
is a continuous version of the process
Z_{t}^{y,n;r,x}
for all
\mathbb{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{K}
.
Define

\displaystyle F_{t}^{\mathbb{y},n,r,x}

\displaystyle=e^{\int_{r}^{t}\nabla_{y}g(s,\mathbb{X}_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x})\mathrm{d}s}\nabla_{x}Y_{t}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}+\int_{r}^{t}e^{\int_{r}^{s}\nabla_{y}g(v,\mathbb{X}_{v}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x})\mathrm{d}v}Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}\nabla_{x}b(s,X_{s}^{r,x})\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{r}^{t}e^{\int_{r}^{s}\nabla_{y}g(v,\mathbb{X}_{v}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x})\mathrm{d}v}\nabla_{x}g(s,X_{s}^{r,x},Y_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x},Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x})\mathrm{d}s.

(4.25)

We notice that for all
t\leq R,

\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}}[F_{R}^{\mathbb{y},n,r,x}/\mathfrak{F}_{t}]=F_{t}^{\mathbb{y},n,r,x}
which implies that the process
F^{\mathbb{y},n,r,x}
is a
\mathbb{Q}\text{-martingale}
. Therefore,
(F_{t}^{\mathbb{y},n,r,x})^{2}
is a
\mathbb{Q}\text{-submartingale}
and the following holds

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}}\int_{t}^{R}|F_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n,r,x}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\geq(R-t)|\nabla_{x}Y_{r}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}|^{2}=(R-t)|Z_{r}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}|^{2}.

(4.26)

Hence, from (
4.25
) there is a constant depending on
\|\nabla b\|_{\infty},\|Z^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}*W\|_{\operatorname{BMO}}
and
e^{\|\nabla_{y}g\|_{\infty}}
such that

\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}}\int_{t}^{R}|F_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n,r,x}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq C
.
Note also that the constant
C
above does not depend on
\|\nabla_{x}G_{n}(\mathbb{y};\cdot)\|_{\infty}
for all
\mathbb{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{K}
. Thus

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}|Z_{r}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}|\leq\frac{C}{(R-r)^{1/2}}.

(4.27)

Hence from
[
36
, Theorem 6.2]
4
4

4

Note that the uniform Lipschitz condition on the forward component of the driver can be relaxed by means of standard approximations
, we observe that the following representation holds for all
r\leq\varrho\leq R
and
\mathbb{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{K}

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}U^{n}(\mathbb{y};r,x)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}}\Big[U^{n}(\mathbb{y};R,X_{R}^{r,x})N_{R}^{r,x}+\int_{r}^{R}\Big(Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}b({s},X_{s}^{r,x})-g({s},X_{s}^{r,x},Y_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x},Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x})\Big)N_{{s}}^{r,x}\mathrm{d}s\Big],

where
N_{s}^{r,x}
stands for the Malliavin weights associated to the forward equation in (
4.23
) under the measure
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
, which is given by

\displaystyle N_{t}^{r,x}=\frac{1}{t-r}\int_{r}^{t}\nabla X_{s}\mathrm{d}\tilde{W}_{s}[\nabla X_{r}]^{-1}.

(4.28)

Moreover, one can show that the process
N_{s}^{r,x}
satisfies the following for all
p\geq 1
and
r\leq t

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\mathbb{E}^{\bar{\mathbb{P}}}[|N_{t}^{r,x}|^{p}]\leq C(t-r)^{-p/2},\\
\mathbb{E}^{\bar{\mathbb{P}}}\left[\sup_{s\in[\frac{R+r}{2},R]}|N_{s}^{r,x}|^{p}\right]\leq C(R-r)^{-p/2}.\end{cases}

(4.29)

Hence, using (
4.20
),(
4.29
), the growth of
g
and the fact that
b
is bounded, we deduce that

\displaystyle|\nabla_{x}U^{n}(y;r,x)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{R-r}}+C\mathbb{E}^{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}}\int_{r}^{\frac{R+r}{2}}\left(1+\|b\|_{\infty}|Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}|+|Y_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}|+\ell(|Y_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}|)|Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}|^{2}\right)|N_{s}^{r,x}|\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\quad+C\mathbb{E}^{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}}\int_{\frac{R+r}{2}}^{R}\left(1+|Y_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}|+(1+\ell(|Y_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}|))|Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}|^{2}\right)|N_{s}^{r,x}|\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\frac{C}{\sqrt{R-r}}+I_{1}+I_{2}.

Using mainly the bound (
4.20
)and (
4.29
), the local boundedness of the function
\ell
and Hölder’s inequality, we obtain the following estimate for
I_{1}
, for all
\mathbb{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{K}

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}^{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}}\int_{r}^{\frac{R+r}{2}}\left((1+|Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}|^{3/2})(1+|Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}|^{1/2})\right)|N_{s}^{r,x}|\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{s\in[r,\frac{R+r}{2}]}(1+|Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}|^{3/2})\mathbb{E}^{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}}\int_{r}^{\frac{R+r}{2}}\left(1+|Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}|^{1/2}\right)|N_{s}^{r,x}|\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(1+\frac{(R-r)^{-3/4}}{2}\Big)\mathbb{E}^{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}}\Big[\int_{r}^{\frac{R+r}{2}}(1+|Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}|^{2})\mathrm{d}s\Big]^{1/4}\mathbb{E}^{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}}\Big[\int_{r}^{\frac{R+r}{2}}|N_{s}^{r,x}|^{4/3}\mathrm{d}s\Big]^{3/4}

\displaystyle\leq C(R-r)^{-3/4}(R-r)^{1/4},

where the constant
C
above does not depend on
n
and not on the Lipschitz constant of the terminal value
G_{n}
.

On the other hand, the bound for
I_{2}
follows by

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}^{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}}[\sup_{s\in[\frac{R+r}{2},R]}|N_{s}^{r,x}|]\int_{\frac{R+r}{2}}^{R}\left(1+|Y_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}|+(1+\ell(|Y_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}|))|Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}|^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}^{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}}\Big[\sup_{s\in[\frac{R+r}{2},R]}|N_{s}^{r,x}|^{q}\Big]^{1/q}\mathbb{E}^{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}}\Big[\Big(\int_{\frac{R+r}{2}}^{R}1+|Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{p}\Big]^{1/p}

\displaystyle\leq C(R-r)^{-1/2}.

Combining the bounds for
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
, we deduce that there is a constant
C
such that for all
\mathbb{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{K}

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}(R-r)^{1/2}|\nabla_{x}U^{n}(\mathbb{y};r,x)|\leq C.

(4.30)

Consequently, we deduce that the function
U^{n}(\mathbb{y},r,x)
is measurable on
\mathbb{R}^{K}\times[r,R]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

The limit
U(\mathbb{y};r,x)
and its gradient
\nabla_{x}U(\mathbb{y};r,x)
:

Let us now establish some properties satisfied by the limit function
U
and its derivative. Applying
[
21
, Lemma 3.8]
, we have

\displaystyle\|Y_{t}^{\mathbb{y};r,x}-U(\mathbb{y};t,x)\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|Y_{t}^{\mathbb{y};r,x}-U^{n}(\mathbb{y};t,x)\|_{2}+\|U^{n}(\mathbb{y};t,x)-U(\mathbb{y};t,x)\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|G_{n}(\mathbb{y},X_{R}^{r,x})-G(\mathbb{y},X_{R}^{r,x})\|^{2}+\|U^{n}(\mathbb{y};t,x)-U(\mathbb{y};t,x)\|_{2}.

Thanks to (
4.20
), we have that
|U(\mathbb{y};t,x)|\leq C
. Applying the dominated convergence theorem and considering the smoothness properties of the transition density of the diffusion process
X^{r,x}
, we conclude that both terms on the right side of the inequality tend to zero as
n
goes to infinity. Consequently,

\displaystyle Y_{t}^{\mathbb{y};r,x}=U(\mathbb{y};t,X_{t}^{r,x})\text{ a.s.},\text{ for all }t\in[r,R].

On the other hand, we notice that the following representation remains valid under the underlying probability measure
\mathbb{P}
:

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}U^{n}(\mathbb{y};r,x)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[G_{n}(\mathbb{y};X_{R}^{r,x})\tilde{N}_{R}^{r,x}+\int_{r}^{R}g({s},X_{s}^{r,x},Y_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x},Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x})\tilde{N}_{{s}}^{r,x}\mathrm{d}s\Big],

where the Malliavin weights
\tilde{N}^{r,x}
is now given by

\displaystyle\tilde{N}_{t}^{r,x}=\frac{1}{t-r}\int_{r}^{t}\nabla X_{s}\mathrm{d}{W}_{s}[\nabla X_{r}]^{-1}.

(4.31)

and
\nabla X_{s}
stands for the first variation process of the diffusion
X
given by (
1.1
) and
[\nabla X_{r}]^{-1}
is its inverse. Applying the dominated convergence theorem, we obtain that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\nabla_{x}U^{n}(\mathbb{y};r,x)

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}\Big[G_{n}(\mathbb{y};X_{R}^{r,x})\tilde{N}_{R}^{r,x}+\int_{r}^{R}g({s},X_{s}^{r,x},Y_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x},Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x})\tilde{N}_{{s}}^{r,x}\mathrm{d}s\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[G(\mathbb{y};X_{R}^{r,x})\tilde{N}_{R}^{r,x}+\int_{r}^{R}g({s},X_{s}^{r,x},Y_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x},Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y},n;r,x})\tilde{N}_{{s}}^{r,x}\mathrm{d}s\Big]:=V(\mathbb{y};r,x).

It follows from (
4.30
) that for all
\mathbb{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{K}

\displaystyle V(\mathbb{y};r,x)\leq C(R-t)^{1/2}.

This implies that for all
\delta\in(0,R)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{r}^{R-\delta}\|\nabla_{x}U^{n}(\mathbb{y};s,X_{s}^{r,x})-V(\mathbb{y};s,X_{s}^{r,x})\|_{2}\mathrm{d}s=0.

Notice also that, from
[
21
]
, we have that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{r}^{R}\|\nabla_{x}U^{n}(\mathbb{y};s,X_{s}^{r,x})-Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y};r,x}\|_{2}\mathrm{d}s=0.

Consequently, for almost every
s\in[r,R)
we have

\displaystyle Z_{s}^{\mathbb{y};r,x}=V(\mathbb{y};s,X_{s}^{r,x}),\,\text{ a.s.}

∎

4.3.
Proof of Theorem
1.9

To prove Theorem
1.9
, we require some auxiliary results on the centered (and explicit) BTZ schemes and their properties within our framework. For ease of reading, their proofs are deferred to the appendix.

4.3.1.
Centered BTZ scheme and some properties

In this section, we revisit some properties of the well known BTZ scheme adapted to our general framework. Specifically, we account for the generator not being uniformly Lipschitz continuous in its backward component, and we address a broader class of nonninearities with quadratic growth.
