To conduct our analysis, we use the scheme from
[
54
]
, whic unlike
[
8
]
proposes a modified version of the BTZ scheme, referred to as the centered BTZ scheme.

Linearisation of the centered BTZ scheme:

Let
\pi:0=t_{0}<t_{1}<\cdots<t_{N}=T
be a partition, where

\delta t_{i}=t_{i+1}-t_{i}
and
h=\sup_{0\leq i\leq N-1}\delta t_{i}
. Let
(H_{i})_{0\leq i\leq N-1}
be a sequence of random vectors such that

•

H_{i}
is a
\mathbb{R}^{d}\text{-valued}
random vectors and is
\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i+1}}\text{-measurable},
for all
0\leq i\leq N-1
;

•

for all
0\leq i\leq N-1
,
H_{i}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[H_{i}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}]=0,\,\,\mathbb{E}[H_{i}(H_{i})^{\mathbb{T}}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}]=\mathbb{E}[H_{i}(H_{i})^{\mathbb{T}}]=\frac{c_{i}}{\delta t_{i}}I_{d\times d}

where
C_{1}<c_{i}<C_{2}
for some constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
independent of
i
.

We will consider the following modified BTZ centered scheme

\displaystyle\begin{cases}Y_{i}^{\pi}=\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{i+1}^{\pi}+G_{i}(\mathbb{E}[Y_{i+1}^{\pi}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}],Z_{i}^{\pi})\delta t_{i}\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\right],\\
Z_{i}^{\pi}=\mathbb{E}[Y_{i+1}^{\pi}H_{i}\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}],\\
Y_{N}^{\pi}=\xi,\,\,\,Z_{N}^{\pi}=0.\end{cases}

(4.32)

To ease the notation, we will denote by
\mathscr{E}[G_{i},\xi,H_{i}]
the solution of the BTZ scheme (
4.32
). Unless otherwise stated, we will omit the superscript
\pi
in the sequel.

Remark 4.3
.

The BTZ scheme (
4.32
) can be rewritten without the conditional expectation as follows

\displaystyle Y_{i}^{\pi}=Y_{i+1}^{\pi}+\delta t_{i}G_{i}(\mathbb{E}[Y_{i+1}^{\pi}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}],Z_{i}^{\pi})-\frac{\delta t_{i}}{c_{i}}(Z_{i}^{\pi})^{\mathbb{T}}\cdot H_{i}-\nu_{i},

(4.33)

where
\nu_{i}
is an
\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i+1}}\text{-measurable}
random variable such that
\mathbb{E}[{\nu}_{i}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}]=\mathbb{E}[{\nu}_{i}H_{i}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}]=0
and
\mathbb{E}[({\nu}_{i})^{2}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}]<\infty.

Assumption 4.4

(Driver locally Lipschitz in
z
and
y
)
.

The functions
G_{i}:\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
are
\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\otimes\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R})\otimes\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\text{-measurable}
and satisfy for some positive constants
\Lambda_{y}
and
\Lambda_{z}(n)
which do not depend on
i
but
\Lambda_{z}(n)
may depend on
n
,

(i)

For all
y,y^{\prime},z,z^{\prime}
and
\alpha_{0}\in[0,1)

|G_{i}(y,z)-G_{i}(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})|\leq\Lambda_{y}(1+|z-z^{\prime}|^{\alpha_{0}})|y-y^{\prime}|+\Lambda_{z}(n)\ell(|y-y^{\prime}|)|z-z^{\prime}|,

with
\ell
being a non-negative and locally bounded function.

(ii)

Recall that
h=\sup_{i}\delta t_{i}
. There is a constant
\epsilon\in]0,1[
which does not depend on
n
and
N
, such that

h\Lambda_{y}+\Big(\sup_{0\leq i\leq N-1}\delta t_{i}|H_{i}|\Big)\ell(0)\Lambda_{z}(n)\leq 1-\epsilon.

Assumption 4.5

(Bounded terminal value)
.

Let the functions
G_{i}:\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
being
\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\otimes\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R})\otimes\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\text{-measurable}
and satisfy Assumption
4.4
for all
1\leq i\leq N
.

\xi\in L^{\infty}(\mathfrak{F}_{T})
and
\sup_{1\leq i\leq N}|G_{i}(0,0)|\leq\Lambda_{0}
, where
\Lambda_{0}
is a positive constant not depending of
N
.

Moreover,
\ell(0)\Lambda_{z}(n)<1
where
\Lambda_{z}(n)
is the Lipschitz constant of
G_{i}
with respect to
z
, for all
1\leq i\leq N
.

Assumption 4.6

(Special quadratic structure)
.

Let the functions
G_{i}:\Omega\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
being
\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\otimes\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R})\otimes\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\text{-measurable}
and satisfy Assumption
4.5
for all
1\leq i\leq N-1
. Furthermore,
for all
y,y^{\prime},z,z^{\prime}
and
\alpha_{0}\in(0,1)

|G_{i}(y,z)-G_{i}(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})|\leq\Lambda_{y}(1+|z-z^{\prime}|^{\alpha_{0}})|y-y^{\prime}|+\Lambda_{z}\Big(1+\ell(|y-y^{\prime}|)(|z|+|z^{\prime}|)\Big)|z-z^{\prime}|.

Notice that, under Assumption
4.6
, the driver
G_{i}
has necessarily of the following growth

\displaystyle|G_{i}(y,z)|\leq\Lambda_{0}+\Lambda_{y}|y|+\Lambda_{z}(|z|+2\ell(|y|)|z|^{2}),

(4.34)

for all
0\leq i\leq N
.

Some important properties satisfied by the centered scheme (
4.32
) need to be assessed under our general set of assumptions. One key property is the comparison theorem, presented in the lemma below. Additionally, we provide a representation result for the difference between two BTZ schemes when the driver satisfies Assumption
4.4
.

In the sequel, we set
\delta Y_{i}=Y_{i}^{1}-Y_{i}^{2}
,
\delta Z_{i}=Z_{i}^{1}-Z_{i}^{2}
and
\delta G_{i}=G_{i}^{1}(Y_{i}^{2},Z_{i}^{2})-G_{i}^{2}(Y_{i}^{2},Z_{i}^{2})
, where
(Y_{i}^{1},Z_{i}^{1})
and
(Y_{i}^{2},Z_{i}^{2})
denote respectively, the solutions to
\mathscr{E}[G_{i}^{1},\xi^{1},H_{i}]
and
\mathscr{E}[G_{i}^{2},\xi^{2},H_{i}]
.

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
(Y_{i}^{1},Z_{i}^{1})
and
(Y_{i}^{2},Z_{i}^{2})
be two solutions to the scheme (
4.32
), respectively, such that
(G_{i}^{1},\xi^{1})
and
(G_{i}^{2},\xi^{2})
satisfy Assumption
4.4
. For
0\leq i\leq n
, let

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{i}:=\prod_{j=i}^{N-1}(1+\delta t_{j}\Gamma_{j}+\delta t_{j}\Pi_{j}\cdot{H}_{j}),

where

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j}:

\displaystyle=\frac{G_{j}^{1}(\mathbb{E}[{Y}_{j+1}^{1}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}],{Z}_{j}^{1})-G_{j}^{1}(\mathbb{E}[{Y}_{j+1}^{2}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}],{Z}_{j}^{1})}{\mathbb{E}[\delta{Y}_{j+1}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}]}{\bf 1}_{\{\mathbb{E}[\delta{Y}_{j+1}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}]\neq 0\}},

\displaystyle\Pi_{j}:

\displaystyle=\frac{G_{j}^{1}(\mathbb{E}[{Y}_{j+1}^{2}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}],{Z}_{j}^{1})-G_{j}^{1}(\mathbb{E}[{Y}_{j+1}^{2}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}],{Z}_{j}^{2})}{|\delta{Z}_{j}|^{2}}\delta{Z}_{j}{\bf 1}_{\{\delta{Z}_{j}\neq 0\}}.

Then the following representation holds

\displaystyle\delta Y_{i}=\mathbb{E}\Big[\Upsilon_{i}\delta Y_{t_{N}}+\sum_{k=i}^{N-1}\delta t_{k}\delta G_{k}\frac{\Upsilon_{i}}{\Upsilon_{k}}\Big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big].

(4.35)

Moreover, the following comparison principle holds true:
Y_{i}^{1}\geq Y_{i}^{2}
,
whenever
Y_{N}^{1}\geq Y_{N}^{2}
and
G_{j}^{1}(Y_{i}^{2},Z_{i}^{2})\geq G_{j}^{2}(Y_{i}^{2},Z_{i}^{2})
.

Proof.

See Appendix
A

∎

Below, we derive another important property satisfied by the centered BTZ scheme (
4.32
) under our framework. This property involves the uniform boundedness of the component
Y_{i}
of the scheme, which plays a key role in ensuring stability and error bounds in our setting.

Lemma 4.8
.

Let Assumption
4.5
be in force. Then

\displaystyle|Y_{i}|\leq(\|\xi\|_{\infty}+\Lambda_{0}T)\exp\left(1+\Lambda_{y}T\right).

Proof.

See Appendix
A

∎

Remark 4.9
.

We emphasize that the bound obtained above does not depend on
\alpha\in(0,1)
, despite the driver being stochastic Lipschitz in
y
. Moreover, this bound is similar to that of the component solution
Y
to (
1.2
) (see, for instance,
[
21
]
).

We conclude this subsection with another important property: we prove that the BMO bound of the control process
Z_{i}
in the BTZ scheme (
4.32
) is preserved under our assumptions.

Lemma 4.10
.

Let Assumption
4.6
be in force and let
(Y_{i}^{\pi},Z_{i}^{\pi})
be the solution associated to
\mathscr{E}[g_{i},\xi,H_{i}]
. Set
Z_{i}^{\pi}(s):=Z_{i}^{\pi}
for all
s\in[t_{i},t_{i+1})
, for all
0\leq i\leq N.
Then, there exists a constant
C>0
not depending on
\pi
such that

\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}{\delta t_{j}}|Z_{j}^{\pi}|^{2}\Big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]\leq C.

Proof.

See Appendix
A

∎

Stability of the explicit BTZ scheme:
Here we study the difference between the scheme given by (
4.32
) and its perturbed version given below.

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\tilde{Y}_{i}=\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{Y}_{i+1}+G_{i}(\mathbb{E}[\tilde{Y}_{i+1}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}],\tilde{Z}_{i})\delta t_{i}\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\right]+\zeta_{i}^{Y},\\
\tilde{Z}_{i}=\mathbb{E}[\tilde{Y}_{i+1}H_{i}\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}],\\
\tilde{Y}_{N}=\xi,\,\,\,\tilde{Z}_{N}=0,\end{cases}

(4.36)

where
(\zeta_{i}^{Y})_{i}
is a family of
\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\text{-measurable}
and square integrable random variables. furthermore, we assume

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq i<N}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}|\tilde{Z}_{j}|^{2}\delta t_{j}\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]<c.

(4.37)

In the sequel, we will adopt the following notations for all
0\leq i\leq N

\displaystyle\tilde{\delta}Y_{i}^{\pi}:=\tilde{Y}_{i}-Y_{i}^{\pi},\quad\tilde{\delta}Z_{i}^{\pi}:=\tilde{Z}_{i}-Z_{i}^{\pi},

and

\displaystyle\tilde{\delta}Y_{i}^{\pi}:=\tilde{\delta}Y^{\pi}(t),\forall t\in[t_{i},t_{i+1}]\quad\tilde{\delta}Z_{i}^{\pi}:=\tilde{\delta}Z^{\pi}(t),\forall t\in[t_{i},t_{i+1}].

Additionally, we introduce the following processes for
0\leq i\leq n
,

\displaystyle\tilde{E}_{i}^{\pi}:=\prod_{j=i}^{N-1}(1+\delta t_{j}\tilde{\Pi}_{j}^{\pi}\cdot{H}_{j}),

and as in Lemma
4.7
, we define

\displaystyle\tilde{\Pi}_{j}^{\pi}:

\displaystyle=\frac{G_{j}(\mathbb{E}[\tilde{Y}_{j+1}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}],\tilde{Z}_{j})-G_{j}(\mathbb{E}[\tilde{Y}_{j+1}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}],{Z}_{j}^{\pi})}{|\tilde{\delta}{Z}_{j}^{\pi}|^{2}}\tilde{\delta}{Z}_{j}^{\pi}{\bf 1}_{\{\tilde{\delta}{Z}_{j}^{\pi}\neq 0\}}.

The processes
\tilde{\Gamma}_{j}^{\pi}
and
\tilde{\Upsilon}_{j}^{\pi}
are also defined in a similar way. Under Assumption
4.4
, for all
1\leq i\leq N-1
we have that
\tilde{E}_{i}^{\pi}>0
and
\mathbb{E}[\tilde{E}_{i}^{\pi}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}]=1
, which implies that the measure
\mathbb{Q}^{\pi}
given by

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{Q}^{\pi}}{\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}}:=\tilde{E}_{i}^{\pi}(t),\quad\tilde{E}_{i}^{\pi}(t):=\prod_{0\leq j\leq i-1}\left(1+\delta t_{j}\tilde{\Pi}_{j}^{\pi}\cdot{H}_{j}\right),

(4.38)

defines a probability measure. The equivalence between the newly introduced probability measure
\mathbb{Q}^{\pi}
and the underlying one
\mathbb{P}
for all
\pi
, is a consequence of the following proposition. This can be derived in our general setting (without any major difficulty, provided that (
4.37
) holds) in a manner similar to the approach in
[
8
, Proposition 2.11]
(see also
[
54
, Proposition 3.3.3]
).

Proposition 4.11
.

Under Assumption
4.6
, the process
M_{t}:=\sum_{t_{i+1}\leq t}\delta t_{i}\tilde{\Pi}_{i}^{\pi}\cdot{H}_{i}
, is a
\operatorname{BMO}
martingale with respect to the filtration
\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}
. Moreover, the Doléans-Dade exponential
\tilde{\Upsilon}_{i}^{\pi}
satisfies the reverse Hölder inequality.

As a by-product of the aforementioned proposition, along with equation (
4.35
), we can derive a more manageable estimate of the difference between the backward components of the scheme (
4.32
) and its perturbed version (
4.36
). This estimate will be described in detail below. A key step in obtaining this representation involves identifying a process
J_{j}^{\epsilon}
such that

\displaystyle\frac{1+\delta t_{j}\tilde{\Gamma}_{j}^{\pi}+\delta t_{j}\tilde{\Pi}_{j}^{\pi}\cdot H_{j}}{1+\delta t_{j}\tilde{\Pi}_{j}^{\pi}\cdot H_{j}}=1+\frac{\delta t_{j}\tilde{\Gamma}_{j}^{\pi}}{1+\delta t_{j}\tilde{\Pi}_{j}^{\pi}\cdot H_{j}}<J_{j}^{\epsilon}.

The superscript
\epsilon
in
J_{j}^{\epsilon}
is kept here, to emphasize the dependence of the process on the constant
\epsilon
which appears in Assumption
4.6
.
An interesting discussion on how to construct the desired process
J_{j}^{\epsilon}
can be found in
[
54
, Page 67]
, and it is readily seen that the same reasoning applies in our setting. Therefore, we choose the process
J_{j}^{\epsilon}
such that
0<J^{\epsilon}_{j}<(1+\Lambda_{y}\delta t_{j})^{\frac{1}{\epsilon}}
. Hence

\displaystyle|\tilde{\delta}Y_{i}^{\pi}|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\Upsilon_{i}|\tilde{\delta}Y_{N}|+\sum_{k=i}^{N-1}|\zeta_{k}^{Y}|\frac{\Upsilon_{i}}{\Upsilon_{k}}\Big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\tilde{E}_{i}^{\pi}\prod_{j=i}^{N-1}J_{j}^{\epsilon}|\tilde{\delta}Y_{N}|+\sum_{k=i}^{N-1}|\zeta_{k}^{Y}|\frac{\tilde{E}_{i}^{\pi}}{\tilde{E}_{k}^{\pi}}\prod_{j=i}^{k-1}J_{j}^{\epsilon}\Big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\exp\big(\frac{\Lambda_{y}h}{\epsilon}\big)\mathbb{E}\Big[\tilde{E}_{i}^{\pi}|\tilde{\delta}Y_{N}|+\sum_{k=i}^{N-1}|\zeta_{k}^{Y}|\frac{\tilde{E}_{i}^{\pi}}{\tilde{E}_{k}^{\pi}}\Big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big].

Using the fact that
\mathbb{E}[\tilde{E}_{k}^{\pi}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{k}}]=1
for all
i\leq k
, we finally obtain that

\displaystyle|\tilde{\delta}Y_{i}^{\pi}|

\displaystyle\leq\exp\big(\frac{\Lambda_{y}h}{\epsilon}\big)\mathbb{E}\Big[\tilde{E}_{i}^{\pi}\Big(|\tilde{\delta}Y_{N}|+\sum_{k=i}^{N-1}|\zeta_{k}^{Y}|\Big)\Big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big].

Thanks to (
4.38
) and applying the reverse Hölder’s inequality, we deduce the following

Lemma 4.12
.

Under Assumption
4.4
, there exist constants
C_{\epsilon}>0
and
q^{*}
independent of
N
such that for all

\displaystyle|\tilde{\delta}Y_{i}^{\pi}|\leq C_{\epsilon}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[|\tilde{\delta}Y_{N}^{\pi}|^{q^{*}}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{q^{*}}}+\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}|\zeta_{j}^{Y}|\Big)^{q^{*}}\Big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{q^{*}}}\Big),

(4.39)

where
q^{*}
is the conjugate arising from the reverse Hölder inequality of Proposition
4.11
and the constant
\epsilon>0
.

We conclude this section with another important result regarding the stability of the component
Z
. We clarify the impact of the perturbation term
\zeta_{i}^{Y}
on the difference between the control process components of the schemes (
4.32
) and (
4.36
), respectively.

Lemma 4.13
.

Let Assumption
4.6
be in force. Then we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\delta t_{i}|\tilde{\delta}Z_{i}^{\pi}|^{2}\Big]\leq C\Big(\mathbb{E}|\tilde{\delta}Y_{N}^{\pi}|^{2}+\mathbb{E}\big[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N-1}|\tilde{\delta}Y_{i}^{\pi}|^{4}\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{i=0}^{N}\tilde{\delta}Y_{i}^{\pi}(\zeta_{i}^{Y})\Big]\Big).

(4.40)

The proof of the above lemma essentially follows the arguments in
[
8
, Proposition 2.12]
(see also
[
54
, Proposition 3.3.4]
). For the sake of completeness, we will briefly reproduce it in the Appendix
A
section below.

4.3.2.
Time-discretization of FBSDEs with singular drifts coefficient

In this subsection, we treat the numerical aspect of the FBSDE (
1.1
)-(
1.2
).

The following result pertains to the error between the BSDE (
1.2
) and the approximated solution (
1.12
). It can be viewed as an extension of
[
21
, Theorem 6]
to the case where the drift of the forward equation is merely bounded and Dini-continuous, with a path-dependent terminal value. We will not reproduce its proof here, as it can be carried out in a manner similar to that in
[
21
, Theorem 6]
, without any significant difficulties. In fact, one of the pivotal estimate for the proof of the theorem is given by (
3.25
).

Theorem 4.14
.

Let Assumption
1.3
be in force. Let
(X,Y,Z)
be the solution to (
1.1
)-(
1.2
) and
(X,Y^{n},Z^{n})
be the solution of (
1.1
)-(
1.12
),
n\geq 1
. Then, for any
p>1
and
\kappa\geq 1
, there exists a positive finite constant
C(p,\kappa)
such that for any
\nu\geq 1
and any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|Y_{t}^{n}-Y_{t}|^{2p}+\Big(\int_{0}^{T}|Z^{n}_{s}-Z_{s}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{p}\Big]\leq{\frac{C(p,\kappa)}{n^{q}}}\wedge\exp(-Cn^{2}).

The perturbed explicit BTZ scheme:

Let us observe that the truncated solution
(Y^{n},Z^{n})
to the BSDE (
1.12
) can be expressed as a perturbed BTZ scheme (
4.36
) as follows: set
\tilde{Y}_{i}:=Y_{t_{i}}^{n}

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\tilde{Y}_{i}^{n}=\mathbb{E}\Big[\tilde{Y}_{i+1}^{n}+g_{n}(t_{i},\mathscr{X}_{i}^{\pi},\mathbb{E}[\tilde{Y}_{i+1}^{n}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}],\tilde{Z}_{i}^{n})\delta t_{i}\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]+\zeta_{i}^{Y},\\
\tilde{Z}_{i}^{n}=\mathbb{E}[\tilde{Y}_{i+1}^{n}H_{i}^{R}\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}],\\
\tilde{Y}_{N}^{n}=\xi^{\pi},\,\,\,\tilde{Z}_{N}^{n}=0,\end{cases}

(4.41)

where

\displaystyle\zeta_{i}^{Y}=\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}g_{n}(s,X_{s},Y_{s}^{n},Z_{s}^{n})-g_{n}(t_{i},\mathscr{X}_{i}^{\pi},\mathbb{E}[\tilde{Y}_{i+1}^{n}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}],\tilde{Z}_{i}^{n})\mathrm{d}s\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big].

(4.42)

The following result plays a key role in the proof of the main error estimates of the BTZ scheme, as it allows the application of the result derived earlier in their proof

Lemma 4.15
.

The perturbed scheme
(\tilde{Y}_{i},\tilde{Z}_{i})
solution to (
4.41
) satisfies for all
0\leq i\leq N-1

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}|\tilde{Z}_{i}^{n}|^{2}\delta t_{j}\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]\leq c.

Proof.

See Appendix
A
.
∎

Remark 4.16
.

We observe that the processes
\hat{Z}^{n}
and
\bar{\bar{Z}}^{n}
, given respectively by (
A.11
) and (
A.11
), are also
\operatorname{BMO}\text{-martingales}
under the underlying probability
\mathbb{P}
. In addition, their
\operatorname{BMO}\text{-norms}
depend on
\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\|Z^{n}*W\|_{\operatorname{BMO}}
.

Remark 4.17
.

We carefully underline that, under Assumption
1.3
, the scheme (
4.41
) satisfies Assumption
4.4
. Moreover, there exists a constant
\epsilon\in(0,1)
that does not depend on
N,n
and
R
, such that

\displaystyle h\Lambda_{y}+\sup_{0\leq i\leq N-1}\delta t_{i}|H_{i}^{R}|\ell(0)\Lambda_{z}\leq 1-\epsilon.

(4.43)

Thanks to Lemmas
4.8
and
4.10
, we deduce that

Corollary 4.18
.

Under Assumption
1.3
and for
N
large enough, the following holds

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq i\leq N}\Big(|\mathscr{Y}^{\pi}_{i}|+\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{k=i}^{N-1}|\mathscr{Z}_{k}^{\pi}|^{2}\delta t_{k}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]\Big)\leq C.

We are now ready to prove Theorem
1.9
which follows from the following propositions

Proposition 4.19
.

Let assumptions of Theorem
1.9
be in force. Then, there exists
q^{*}>1
such that for all
p\geq 1

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N}|Y_{t_{i}}-\mathscr{Y}_{i}^{\pi}|^{2p}\Big]\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[|\Phi(X)-\Phi(\mathscr{X}^{\pi})|^{2pq^{*}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{q^{*}}}+C_{p}\sup_{0\leq i\leq N-1}\mathbb{E}[|X_{t_{i}}-\mathscr{X}_{i}^{\pi}|^{4pq^{*}}]^{\frac{1}{2q^{*}}}+Ch^{p}.

Proposition 4.20
.

Under the conditions of Theorem
1.9
there exists
q^{*}>1
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{T}|Z_{s}-\mathscr{Z}^{\pi}(s)|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big]\leq C\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[|\Phi(X)-\Phi(\mathscr{X}^{\pi})|^{8q^{*}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{8q^{*}}}h^{\frac{1}{2}}+\sup_{0\leq j\leq N-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X_{t_{j}}-\mathscr{X}_{j}^{\pi}|^{16q^{*}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{16q^{*}}}h^{\frac{1}{2}}+h\Big).

4.3.3.
Proof of Proposition
4.19
and Proposition
4.20

Proof of Proposition
4.19
.

We first notice that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N}|Y_{t_{i}}-\mathscr{Y}_{i}^{\pi}|^{2p}\Big]\leq C(p)\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N}|Y_{t_{i}}-{Y}_{t_{i}}^{n}|^{2p}\Big]+\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N}|{Y}_{t_{i}}^{n}-\mathscr{Y}_{i}^{\pi}|^{2p}\Big]\Big).

Thanks to Theorem
4.14
and keep in mind that
\alpha\in(0,1/2)
and
h=n^{-\alpha}
, with
h
small enough, we deduce that

\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N}|Y_{t_{i}}-{Y}_{t_{i}}^{n}|^{2p}\Big]\leq C_{\alpha,p}h^{p}.

Let us now turn to the control between the truncated solution
Y^{n}
and
\mathscr{Y}^{\pi}
. We first notice that the perturbed term
\zeta_{i}^{Y}
given in (
4.42
), can be rewritten as:

\displaystyle\zeta_{i}^{Y}:=\mathbb{E}\Big[\zeta_{i}^{Y,t}+\zeta_{i}^{Y,x}+\zeta_{i}^{Y,y}+\zeta_{i}^{Y,y,\mathbb{E}}+\zeta_{i}^{Y,\hat{z}}+\zeta_{i}^{Y,\bar{\bar{z}}}+\zeta_{i}^{Y,\tilde{z}}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big],

(4.44)

where

\displaystyle\zeta_{i}^{Y,t}:=

\displaystyle\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}g_{n}(s,X_{s},Y_{s}^{n},Z_{s}^{n})-g_{n}(t_{i},X_{s},Y_{s}^{n},Z_{s}^{n})\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\zeta_{i}^{Y,x}:=

\displaystyle\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}g_{n}(t_{i},X_{s},Y_{s}^{n},Z_{s}^{n})-g_{n}(t_{i},\mathscr{X}_{i}^{\pi},Y_{s}^{n},Z_{s}^{n})\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\zeta_{i}^{Y,y}:=

\displaystyle\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}g_{n}(t_{i},\mathscr{X}_{i}^{\pi},Y_{s}^{n},Z_{s}^{n})-g_{n}(t_{i},\mathscr{X}_{i}^{\pi},Y_{t_{i}}^{n},Z_{s}^{n})\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\zeta_{i}^{Y,y,\mathbb{E}}:=

\displaystyle\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}g_{n}(t_{i},\mathscr{X}_{i}^{\pi},Y_{t_{i}}^{n},Z_{s}^{n})-g_{n}(t_{i},\mathscr{X}_{i}^{\pi},\mathbb{E}[\tilde{Y}_{i+1}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}],Z_{s}^{n})\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\zeta_{i}^{Y,\hat{z}}:=

\displaystyle\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}g_{n}(t_{i},\mathscr{X}_{i}^{\pi},\mathbb{E}[\tilde{Y}_{i+1}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}],Z_{s}^{n})-g_{n}(t_{i},\mathscr{X}_{i}^{\pi},\mathbb{E}[\tilde{Y}_{i+1}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}],\hat{Z}_{i}^{n})\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\zeta_{i}^{Y,\bar{\bar{z}}}:=

\displaystyle\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}g_{n}(t_{i},\mathscr{X}_{i}^{\pi},\mathbb{E}[\tilde{Y}_{i+1}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}],\hat{Z}_{i}^{n})-g_{n}(t_{i},\mathscr{X}_{i}^{\pi},\mathbb{E}[\tilde{Y}_{i+1}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}],\bar{\bar{Z}}_{i}^{n})\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\zeta_{i}^{Y,\tilde{z}}:=

\displaystyle\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}g_{n}(t_{i},\mathscr{X}_{i}^{\pi},\mathbb{E}[\tilde{Y}_{i+1}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}],\bar{\bar{Z}}_{i}^{n})-g_{n}(t_{i},\mathscr{X}_{i}^{\pi},\mathbb{E}[\tilde{Y}_{i+1}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}],\tilde{Z}_{i}^{n})\mathrm{d}s.

Applying Lemma
4.12
and
[
8
, Remark 2.5]
to the terms
\zeta_{i}^{Y,x},\zeta_{i}^{Y,y},\zeta_{i}^{Y,\bar{\bar{z}}}
and
\zeta_{i}^{Y,\tilde{z}}
, we deduce that

\displaystyle|Y_{t_{i}}^{n}-\mathscr{Y}_{i}^{\pi}|

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\left[|Y_{t_{N}}^{n}-\mathscr{Y}_{N}^{\pi}|^{q^{*}}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\right]^{\frac{1}{q^{*}}}+C\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\zeta_{j}^{Y,x}\Big)^{q^{*}}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{q^{*}}}+C\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\zeta_{j}^{Y,y}\Big)^{q^{*}}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{q^{*}}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\zeta_{j}^{Y,\bar{\bar{z}}}\Big)^{q^{*}}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{q^{*}}}+C\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\zeta_{j}^{Y,\tilde{z}}\Big)^{q^{*}}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{q^{*}}}+C\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{\pi}}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\big|\zeta_{j}^{Y,t}\big|/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+C\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{\pi}}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\big|\zeta_{j}^{Y,\hat{z}}\big|/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]+C\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{\pi}}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\big|\zeta_{j}^{Y,y,\mathbb{E}}\big|/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big].

Taking the
2p
-th power, the supremum on both sides, and applying the Jensen and Doob’s maximal inequalities, we obtain for all
p\geq 2

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N}|Y_{t_{i}}^{n}-\mathscr{Y}_{i}^{\pi}|^{2p}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\mathbb{E}\left[|Y_{t_{N}}^{n}-\mathscr{Y}_{N}^{\pi}|^{2pq^{*}}\right]^{\frac{1}{q^{*}}}+C_{p}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\big|\zeta_{j}^{Y,x}\big|\Big)^{2pq^{*}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{q^{*}}}+C_{p}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\big|\zeta_{j}^{Y,y}\big|\Big)^{2pq^{*}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{q^{*}}}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{p}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\big|\zeta_{j}^{Y,\bar{\bar{z}}}\big|\Big)^{2pq^{*}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{q^{*}}}+C_{p}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\big|\zeta_{j}^{Y,\tilde{z}}\big|\Big)^{2pq^{*}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{q^{*}}}+C_{p}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{\pi}}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\big|\zeta_{j}^{Y,t}\big|/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]^{2p}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad+C_{p}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{\pi}}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\big|\zeta_{j}^{Y,\hat{z}}\big|/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]^{2p}\Big]+C_{p}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{\pi}}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\big|\zeta_{j}^{Y,y,\mathbb{E}}\big|/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]^{2p}\Big].

Let us analyse separately the different terms that appear on the right side of the above inequality. By definition, the first term corresponds to the error analysis of the terminal value. To estimate the second term, we primarily use the fact that the generator is stochastic Lipschitz continuous in
x
and apply Hölder’s inequality to deduce that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\big|\zeta_{j}^{Y,x}\big|\Big)^{2pq^{*}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}(1+\ell(|Y_{s}^{n}|)|Z_{s}^{n}|^{\alpha})|X_{s}-\mathscr{X}_{j}^{\pi}|\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2pq^{*}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\sup_{t_{j}\leq s\leq t_{j+1}}(1+\ell(|Y_{s}^{n}|)|Z_{s}^{n}|^{\alpha})|X_{s}-\mathscr{X}_{j}^{\pi}|\delta t_{j}\Big)^{2pq^{*}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\sup_{t_{j}\leq s\leq t_{j+1}}(1+\ell(|Y_{s}^{n}|)|Z_{s}^{n}|^{\alpha})^{2pq^{*}}|X_{s}-\mathscr{X}_{j}^{\pi}|^{2pq^{*}}\delta t_{j}\Big]\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\delta t_{j}\Big)^{2pq^{*}-1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}T^{2pq^{*}}\sup_{0\leq j\leq N-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{t_{j}\leq s\leq t_{j+1}}(1+\ell(|Y_{s}^{n}|)|Z_{s}^{n}|^{\alpha})^{2pq^{*}}|X_{s}-\mathscr{X}_{j}^{\pi}|^{2pq^{*}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}T^{2pq^{*}}\sup_{0\leq j\leq N-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{t_{j}\leq s\leq t_{j+1}}(1+\ell(|Y_{s}^{n}|)|Z_{s}^{n}|^{\alpha})^{4pq^{*}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X_{s}-\mathscr{X}_{j}^{\pi}|^{4pq^{*}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

From (
3.25
) and the uniform bound of
Y^{n}
we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\big|\zeta_{j}^{Y,x}\big|\Big)^{2pq^{*}}

\displaystyle\leq C(p,q^{*})\Big(\sup_{0\leq j\leq N-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{t_{j}\leq s\leq t_{j+1}}|X_{s}-{X}_{t_{j}}|^{4pq^{*}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\sup_{0\leq j\leq N-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X_{t_{j}}-\mathscr{X}_{j}^{\pi}|^{4pq^{*}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big).

Under our assumptions, we observe that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq j\leq N-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{t_{j}\leq s\leq t_{j+1}}|X_{s}-{X}_{t_{j}}|^{2pq^{*}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C_{pq^{*}}\sup_{0\leq j\leq N-1}\Big(\mathbb{E}\sup_{t_{j}\leq s\leq t_{j+1}}\Big|\int_{t_{j}}^{s}b(v,X_{v})\mathrm{d}v\Big|^{4pq^{*}}+\mathbb{E}\sup_{t_{j}\leq s\leq t_{j+1}}|W_{s}-W_{t_{j}}|^{4pq^{*}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{pq^{*}}h^{2pq^{*}}.

Therefore, there exists a constant
C_{pq^{*}}
not depending on
h
, such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\big|\zeta_{j}^{Y,x}\big|\Big)^{2pq^{*}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{q^{*}}}\leq C_{pq^{*}}h^{p}+\sup_{0\leq j\leq N-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[|X_{t_{j}}-\mathscr{X}_{j}^{\pi}|^{4pq^{*}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2q^{*}}}.

Similarly, from the growth of
g_{n}
and applying Hölder’s inequality, we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\big|\zeta_{j}^{Y,y}\big|\Big)^{2pq^{*}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{q^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|Y_{s}^{n}-{Y}_{t_{j}}^{n}|\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2pq^{*}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{q^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{p}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\sup_{t_{j}\leq s\leq t_{j+1}}|Y_{s}^{n}-{Y}_{t_{j}}^{n}|^{2pq^{*}}\delta t_{j}\Big]^{\frac{1}{q^{*}}}\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\delta t_{j}\Big)^{\frac{2pq^{*}-1}{q^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{pq^{*}}T^{2p}\Big(\sup_{0\leq j\leq N-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{t_{j}\leq s\leq t_{j+1}}|Y_{s}^{n}-{Y}_{t_{j}}^{n}|^{2pq^{*}}\Big]\Big)^{\frac{1}{q^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{pq^{*}}h^{p},

where, we used Theorem
1.4
to obtain the last inequality.
From our assumptions on the driver, we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\big|\zeta_{j}^{Y,\bar{\bar{z}}}\big|\Big)^{2pq^{*}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{q^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}\big(1+\ell(0)(|\hat{Z}^{n}(s)|+|\bar{\bar{Z}}^{n}(s)|)\big)|\hat{Z}^{n}(s)-\bar{\bar{Z}}^{n}(s)|\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2pq^{*}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{q^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}\big(1+\ell(0)(|\hat{Z}^{n}(s)|+|\bar{\bar{Z}}^{n}(s)|)\big)^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2pq^{*}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2q^{*}}}

\displaystyle\times\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|\hat{Z}^{n}(s)-\bar{\bar{Z}}^{n}(s)|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2pq^{*}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2q^{*}}},

(4.45)

where we used the Hölder’s inequality and recall that
\hat{Z}^{n}(s)
and
\bar{\bar{Z}}^{n}(s)
are respectively given by (
A.8
) and (
A.11
).
We first observe that, by performing some elementary computations and then applying the Hölder’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle|\bar{\bar{Z}}^{n}(s)-\hat{Z}^{n}(s)|\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}g_{n}(s,X_{s},Y_{s}^{n},Z_{s}^{n})\mathrm{d}s\frac{W_{t_{j+1}}-W_{t_{j}}}{\delta t_{j}}\Big|\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}g_{n}(s,X_{s},Y_{s}^{n},Z_{s}^{n})\mathrm{d}s\Big|^{2}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\frac{W_{t_{j+1}}-W_{t_{j}}}{\delta t_{j}}\Big|^{2}\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{\sqrt{\delta t_{j}}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}1+|Z_{s}^{n}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sqrt{\delta t_{j}}+\frac{C}{\sqrt{\delta t_{j}}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|Z_{s}^{n}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2}\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}},

(4.46)

where, we used the growth of
g_{n}
, the bound of
Y^{n}
and applied the conditional BDG inequality. Hence, squaring the above inequality, we deduce that

\displaystyle|\bar{\bar{Z}}^{n}(s)-\hat{Z}^{n}(s)|^{2}\leq C{\delta t_{j}}+\frac{C}{{\delta t_{j}}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|Z_{s}^{n}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2}\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}\Big].

Hence, by applying successively the Hölder and Jensen inequalities, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|\hat{Z}^{n}(s)-\bar{\bar{Z}}^{n}(s)|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2pq^{*}}

\displaystyle\leq C(p,q^{*},T)h^{2pq^{*}}+C(p,q^{*})\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\frac{1}{\delta t_{j}^{2pq^{*}}}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|Z_{s}^{n}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{4pq^{*}}\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}\Big]\Big)\delta t_{j}\Big]\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\delta t_{j}\Big)^{2pq^{*}-1}.

This leads to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|\hat{Z}^{n}(s)-\bar{\bar{Z}}^{n}(s)|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big]^{2pq^{*}}

\displaystyle\leq C(p,q^{*},T)h^{2pq^{*}}+C(p,q^{*})T^{2pq^{*}-1}\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\frac{\delta t_{j}}{\delta t_{j}^{2pq^{*}}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|Z_{s}^{n}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{4pq^{*}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C(p,q^{*},T)h^{2pq^{*}}+C(p,q^{*})T^{2pq^{*}-1}\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\frac{\delta t_{j}}{\delta t_{j}^{2pq^{*}}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{t_{j}\leq s\leq t_{j+1}}|Z_{s}^{n}|^{4pq^{*}}(\delta t_{j})^{4pq^{*}}\Big].

From (
3.25
), there exists a constant
C(p,q^{*},T)>0
that does not depend on
N
, such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|\hat{Z}^{n}(s)-\bar{\bar{Z}}^{n}(s)|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2pq^{*}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2q^{*}}}\leq C(p,q^{*},T)h^{p}.

(4.47)

On the other hand, we have seen that the processes
\bar{\bar{Z}}^{n}_{j}
and
\hat{Z}^{n}_{j}
belong to
\mathscr{H}_{BMO}
(see Remark
4.16
). Therefore, uniformly in
n
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}\big(1+\ell(0)(|\hat{Z}^{n}(s)|+|\bar{\bar{Z}}^{n}(s)|)\big)^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2pq^{*}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C(p,q^{*},T)\|(1+\ell(0)(|\hat{Z}^{n}(s)|+|\bar{\bar{Z}}^{n}(s)|)\big)\|^{4pq^{*}}_{\mathscr{H}_{BMO}}.

Similarly, one can also obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\big|\zeta_{j}^{Y,{\tilde{z}}}\big|\Big)^{2pq^{*}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{q^{*}}}\leq C(p,q^{*},T)h^{p}.

(4.48)

In fact, from the growth of
g_{n}
and by applying Hölder’s inequality, we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\big|\zeta_{j}^{Y,\tilde{z}}\big|\Big)^{2pq^{*}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{q^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}\big(1+\ell(0)(|\tilde{Z}^{n}(s)|+|\bar{\bar{Z}}^{n}(s)|)\big)|\tilde{Z}^{n}(s)-\bar{\bar{Z}}^{n}(s)|\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2pq^{*}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{q^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq C(p,q^{*},T)\|(1+\ell(0)(|\hat{Z}^{n}(s)|+|\bar{\bar{Z}}^{n}(s)|)\big)\|^{4pq^{*}}_{\mathscr{H}_{BMO}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|\tilde{Z}^{n}(s)-\bar{\bar{Z}}^{n}(s)|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2pq^{*}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2q^{*}}},

where,
\tilde{Z}^{n}(s):=\tilde{Z}^{n}_{j}
for all
s\in[t_{j},t_{j+1}]
,
\tilde{Z}_{j}^{n}
is given by
(
4.41
)
and belongs to
\mathscr{H}_{BMO}
, thanks to Lemma
4.15
.
On the other hand, from the definitions of the processes
\tilde{Z}^{n}
and
\bar{\bar{Z}}^{n}
, respectively, we obtain that

\displaystyle|\tilde{Z}_{j}^{n}(s)-\bar{\bar{Z}}_{j}^{n}(s)|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[(Y^{n}_{t_{j+1}}-Y^{n}_{t_{j}})\Big(H_{i}^{R}-\frac{W_{t_{j+1}}-W_{t_{j}}}{\delta t_{j}}\Big)\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[|Y^{n}_{t_{j+1}}-Y^{n}_{t_{j}}|^{2}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}\Big]\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(H_{i}^{R}-\frac{W_{t_{j+1}}-W_{t_{j}}}{\delta t_{j}}\Big)^{2}\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|g_{n}(s,X_{s},Y_{s}^{n},Z_{s}^{n})|\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2}+\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|Z_{s}^{n}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}\Big]\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(H_{i}^{R}-\frac{W_{t_{j+1}}-W_{t_{j}}}{\delta t_{j}}\Big)^{2}\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\mathbb{E}\Big[(\delta t_{j})^{2}+\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|Z_{s}^{n}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2}+\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|Z_{s}^{n}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}\Big]\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(H_{i}^{R}-\frac{W_{t_{j+1}}-W_{t_{j}}}{\delta t_{j}}\Big)^{2}\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}\Big],

where, we applied the Hölder’s inequality, and used the growth of
g_{n}
and the uniform boundedness of
Y^{n}
to derive the above inequalities.
Using the definition of the coefficients
H^{R}_{i}
given by (
1.10
), we observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(H_{j}^{R}-\frac{W_{t_{j+1}}-W_{t_{j}}}{\delta t_{j}}\Big)^{2}\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}\Big]\leq\frac{R^{2}}{\delta t_{j}}.

Therefore, there exists a constant
C>0
independent of
n
such that

\displaystyle|\tilde{Z}_{j}^{n}(s)-\bar{\bar{Z}}_{j}^{n}(s)|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\Big[\delta t_{j}+\frac{1}{\delta t_{j}}\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|Z_{s}^{n}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2}+\frac{1}{\delta t_{j}}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|Z_{s}^{n}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}\Big].

Hence, applying the Jensen and Hölder’s inequalities, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|\tilde{Z}^{n}(s)-\bar{\bar{Z}}^{n}(s)|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2pq^{*}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\delta t_{j}\Big[(\delta t_{j})^{2pq^{*}}+\frac{1}{(\delta t_{j})^{2pq^{*}}}\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|Z_{s}^{n}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{4pq^{*}}+\frac{1}{(\delta t_{j})^{2pq^{*}}}\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|Z_{s}^{n}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2pq^{*}}\Big]\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\delta t_{j}\Big)^{2pq^{*}-1}.

Thanks to (
3.25
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|\tilde{Z}^{n}(s)-\bar{\bar{Z}}^{n}(s)|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2pq^{*}}\Big]\leq CT^{2pq^{*}}\big(h+h^{2pq^{*}}\big).

(4.49)

The following bound is a consequence the regularity of the generator on its time variable

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{\pi}}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\big|\zeta_{j}^{Y,t}\big|/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]^{2p}\Big]\leq\sup_{0\leq i\leq N}\Big(\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\Lambda_{t}(\delta t_{j})^{3/2}\Big)^{2p}\leq C(p,T)h^{p}.

(4.50)

Let us now turn to establishing the bounds for the remaining two terms. Using the local Lipschitz assumption of the driver
g
, we first observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{\pi}}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\big|\zeta_{j}^{Y,\hat{z}}\big|/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{\pi}}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}(1+\ell(0)(|\hat{Z}^{n}(s)|+|Z_{s}^{n}|))|\hat{Z}^{n}(s)-{Z}^{n}_{s}|\mathrm{d}s\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C\|(1+\ell(0)(|\hat{Z}^{n}(s)|+|Z_{s}^{n}|))\|_{\mathscr{H}_{BMO}}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{\pi}}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|\hat{Z}^{n}(s)-{Z}^{n}_{s}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

By applying Corollary
1.6
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{\pi}}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\big|\zeta_{j}^{Y,\hat{z}}\big|/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]^{2p}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{\pi}}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|\hat{Z}^{n}(s)-{Z}^{n}_{s}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]^{p}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq Ch^{p}.

Finally, using the growth of
g_{n}
once more, we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{\pi}}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\big|\zeta_{j}^{Y,y,\mathbb{E}}\big|\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda_{y}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{\pi}}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|Y_{t_{j}}-\mathbb{E}[\tilde{Y}_{j+1}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}]|\mathrm{d}s\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda_{y}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{\pi}}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\delta t_{j}\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|g_{n}(s,X_{s},Y_{s}^{n},Z_{s}^{n})|\mathrm{d}s/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}\Big]\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{\pi}}\Big[\int_{t_{i}}^{T}(1+\ell(|Y_{s}^{n}|)|Z_{s}^{n}|^{2})\mathrm{d}s\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]h

\displaystyle\leq C(\epsilon,\|Z^{n}*W\|_{\operatorname{BMO}})h.

Hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N}\mathbb{E}^{\mathbb{Q}^{\pi}}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\big|\zeta_{j}^{Y,y,\mathbb{E}}\big|\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]^{2p}\Big]\leq C(\epsilon,p,\|Z^{n}*W\|_{\operatorname{BMO}})h^{2p}.

∎

Proof of Proposition
4.20
.

We first observe that

\displaystyle|Z_{s}-\mathscr{Z}^{\pi}(s)|\leq|Z_{s}-Z_{s}^{n}|+|Z_{s}^{n}-\hat{Z}_{s}^{n}|+|\hat{Z}_{s}^{n}-\bar{\bar{Z}}^{n}(s)|+|\bar{\bar{Z}}^{n}(s)-\tilde{Z}^{n}(s)|+|\tilde{Z}^{n}(s)-\mathscr{Z}^{\pi}(s)|.

Hence, after performing the necessary computations, we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|Z_{s}-\mathscr{Z}^{\pi}(s)|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big]\leq C\left(II_{1}+II_{2}+II_{3}+II_{4}+II_{5}\right),

where, each term
(II_{l})_{1\leq l\leq 5}
can be deduced easily. It follows from Theorem
4.14
that
II_{1}\leq C(T){h}
. Observe that

\displaystyle|Z_{s}^{n}-\hat{Z}_{s}^{n}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2|Z_{s}^{n}-{Z}_{t_{i}}^{n}|^{2}+2\Big|{Z}_{t_{i}}^{n}-\frac{1}{\delta t_{i}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}{Z}_{v}^{n}\mathrm{d}v/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2|Z_{s}^{n}-{Z}_{t_{i}}^{n}|^{2}+\frac{2}{\delta t_{i}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}|{Z}_{v}^{n}-{Z}_{t_{i}}^{n}|^{2}\mathrm{d}v/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big].

Thanks to Theorem
1.4
, we deduce that

II_{2}\leq 4\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}|Z_{s}^{n}-\hat{Z}_{s}^{n}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big]\leq Ch.

Similar to (
4.47
), there exists a constant
C>0
, such that
II_{3}\leq Ch
. Moreover, from (
4.49
), we also obtain that
II_{4}\leq C(1+h)
.
Let us turn now to the bound for
II_{5}
, whose proof mainly relies on the application of Lemma
4.13
. Indeed, recall that the expression for
\zeta_{j}^{Y}
is given by (
4.44
), and applying the result from Proposition
4.19
, we obtain

\displaystyle II_{5}=\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\delta t_{j}|\tilde{Z}^{n}(s)-\mathscr{Z}^{\pi}(s)|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}|\Phi(X)-\Phi(\mathscr{X}^{\pi})|^{2}+\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq j\leq N-1}|\tilde{Y}_{t_{j}}^{n}-\mathscr{Y}_{j}^{\pi}|^{4}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}(\tilde{Y}_{t_{j}}^{n}-\mathscr{Y}_{j}^{\pi})\zeta_{j}^{Y}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[|\Phi(X)-\Phi(\mathscr{X}^{\pi})|^{4q^{*}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2q^{*}}}+C_{p}\sup_{0\leq j\leq N-1}\mathbb{E}[|X_{t_{j}}-\mathscr{X}_{j}^{\pi}|^{8q^{*}}]^{\frac{1}{4q^{*}}}+Ch+\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}(\tilde{Y}_{t_{j}}^{n}-\mathscr{Y}_{j}^{\pi})\zeta_{j}^{Y}\Big],

The last term on right side of the above inequality can be estimated as follows

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}(\tilde{Y}_{t_{j}}^{n}-\mathscr{Y}_{j}^{\pi})\zeta_{j}^{Y}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}|(\tilde{Y}_{t_{j}}^{n}-\mathscr{Y}_{j}^{\pi})\mathbb{E}\Big[\zeta_{j}^{Y,t}+\zeta_{j}^{Y,x}+\zeta_{j}^{Y,y}+\zeta_{j}^{Y,y,\mathbb{E}}+\zeta_{i}^{Y,\hat{z}}+\zeta_{i}^{Y,\bar{\bar{z}}}+\zeta_{i}^{Y,\tilde{z}}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]\Big|\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{j}|\tilde{Y}_{t_{j}}^{n}-\mathscr{Y}_{j}^{\pi}|^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\big|\zeta_{j}^{Y,t}+\zeta_{j}^{Y,x}+\zeta_{j}^{Y,y}+\zeta_{j}^{Y,y,\mathbb{E}}+\zeta_{j}^{Y,\tilde{z}}+\zeta_{j}^{Y,\bar{\bar{z}}}\big|\Big)^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\Big|\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}(\tilde{Y}_{t_{j}}^{n}-\mathscr{Y}_{j}^{\pi})\zeta_{j}^{Y,\hat{z}}\Big|,

where we used the Hölder’s inequality to obtain the last inequality. We notice that, the second term on the right-hand side of the latter inequality can be controlled in the same manner as done, for instance, in the proof of the previous Proposition. It can be shown that all such terms are bounded by
Ch^{\frac{1}{2}}
, where the constant
C>0
does not depend on
n
. We now turn to deriving the estimate of the third one. Indeed, using the growth of the driver
g
and the local boundedness of the function
\ell
, we obtain the existence of a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}(\tilde{Y}_{t_{j}}^{n}-\mathscr{Y}_{j}^{\pi})\zeta_{j}^{Y,\hat{z}}\Big|\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}\big|\tilde{Y}^{n}(s)-\mathscr{Y}^{\pi}(s)\big|\big(1+\ell(|Y_{s}^{n}|)(|{Z}_{s}^{n}|+|\hat{Z}_{s}^{n}|)\big)|{Z}_{s}^{n}-\hat{Z}_{s}^{n}|\mathrm{d}s\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{t_{i}}^{T}|\tilde{Y}^{n}(s)-\mathscr{Y}^{\pi}(s)|\big(1+\ell(|Y_{s}^{n}|)(|{Z}_{s}^{n}|+|\hat{Z}_{s}^{n}|)\big)|{Z}_{s}^{n}-\hat{Z}_{s}^{n}|\mathrm{d}s\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]\Big].

We recall that the processes
{Z}_{s}^{n}
and
\hat{Z}_{s}^{n}
are both elements of
\mathscr{H}_{BMO}
and applying the Hölder’s inequality, we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}(\tilde{Y}_{t_{j}}^{n}-\mathscr{Y}_{j}^{\pi})\zeta_{j}^{Y,\hat{z}}\Big|\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N-1}\mathbb{E}\big[\sup_{0\leq j\leq N-1}|\tilde{Y}_{j}^{n}-\mathscr{Y}_{j}^{\pi}|^{4}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\big]^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t_{i}}^{T}\big|{Z}_{s}^{n}-\hat{Z}_{s}^{n}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N-1}\mathbb{E}\big[\sup_{0\leq j\leq N-1}|\tilde{Y}_{j}^{n}-\mathscr{Y}_{j}^{\pi}|^{4}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{t_{i}}^{T}\big|{Z}_{s}^{n}-\hat{Z}_{s}^{n}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N-1}\mathbb{E}\big[\sup_{0\leq j\leq N-1}|\tilde{Y}_{j}^{n}-\mathscr{Y}_{j}^{\pi}|^{4}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\big]^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{8}}h^{\frac{1}{2}},

where we used Corollary
1.6
to deduce the last inequality. Therefore, applying the Doob’s maximal inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}(\tilde{Y}_{t_{j}}^{n}-\mathscr{Y}_{j}^{\pi})\zeta_{j}^{Y,\hat{z}}\Big|\Big]\leq 4C\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{0\leq j\leq N-1}|\tilde{Y}_{j}^{n}-\mathscr{Y}_{j}^{\pi}|^{8}\right]^{\frac{1}{8}}h^{\frac{1}{2}}.

∎

5.
Conclusion and some perspectives

Summary of findings:

In this paper, we establish for the first time the rate of convergence for the time discretization of BSDEs coupled with non smooth-type forward SDEs. By incorporating more complex behaviors and relaxing traditional regularity assumptions, our work significantly enhances the understanding of forward-backward SDEs (FBSDEs) in cases where the coefficients of the forward process demonstrates irregularities. This advancement fills a crucial gap in the literature, providing a more rigorous framework for analyzing and approximating FBSDEs under challenging conditions. These findings hold particular importance for potential applications that demand precise numerical approximations, such as those in finance, physics, and stochastic control, where the interplay between backward and forward processes is complex and existing methods often prove inadequate. Our approach not only broadens the scope of FBSDE models but also lays the groundwork for future research into more efficient and accurate computational techniques in systems driven by non-smooth dynamics.

Numerical scheme– An open problem:

However, the BTZ scheme exposed in this work remains very theoretical since it assumes an accurate computations of the conditional expectations involved in the model. For this reason it would have been interesting to develop a fully implementable numerical scheme by following the works
[
14
,
8
,
54
]
in our context. The main ingredient consists to use the quantization method which provides an algorithm to compute expectation appearing in (
4.32
) by establishing a grid associated with probability based on the distribution of the random variables
(\Delta W_{i})
. Precisely, these random variables are approximated by a sequence of centered random variables
(\Delta\hat{W}_{i}=\sqrt{\delta t_{i}}G_{M}(\frac{\Delta W_{i}}{\sqrt{\delta t_{i}}}))
with discrete support, and
G_{M}
stands for the projection operator on the optimal quantization grid for the standard Gaussian distribution with
M
points in the support and the following bound holds for all
p\geq 1

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|\Delta W_{t_{i}}-\Delta\hat{W}_{t_{i}}|^{p}\right]^{{1}/{p}}\leq C_{p}\sqrt{h_{i}}M^{-1/d}.

(5.1)

Henceforth, this necessitates the development of a numerical scheme for the forward equation (
1.1
) using the Markovian quantization method, particularly when the drift term lies outside the standard Lipschitz continuous framework. To the best of our knowledge, no such result currently exists, as it requires a more advanced stability analysis result of the Euler-Maruyama scheme for SDEs with non-smooth coefficients than the one established for instance in the aforementioned Corollary
2.10
(or equivalently in
[
16
]
, for integrable drift coefficients).

In fact, let us consider the following tho version of the Euler scheme approximating the equation (
1.1
) under the assumption (A1)

\displaystyle\mathbb{X}_{i+1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{X}_{i}+b(t_{i},\mathbb{X}_{i})h_{i}+\Delta W_{t_{i}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{Y}_{i+1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{Y}_{i}+b(t_{i},\mathbb{Y}_{i})h_{i}+\Delta W_{t_{i}}+\xi_{i},

where
\xi_{i}
stands for a family of random variables such that for all
i
we have
\xi_{i}\in L^{2p}(\Omega)
. The second equation can be viewed as a perturbation of the first one. Standard computations lead to the following for all
p\geq 2

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\sup_{0\leq k\leq i}|\mathbb{X}_{k}-\mathbb{Y}_{k}|^{p}]\leq C(p)|\mathbb{X}_{0}-\mathbb{Y}_{0}|+C(p)\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sup_{0\leq k\leq i}\Big|\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}\Big(b(t_{j},\mathbb{X}_{j})-b(t_{j},\mathbb{Y}_{j})\Big)h_{j}\Big|^{p}\Bigg]+C(p)\mathbb{E}\Big|\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}\xi_{j}\Big|^{p}.

Since the drift
b
here is not Lipschitz continuous, this prevents us to apply the a Gronwall-type inequality as it was done for instance in
[
8
]
,
[
54
]
and
[
53
]
. Therefore, by just using the boundedness of
b
we deduce the following bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\sup_{0\leq k\leq i}|\mathbb{X}_{k}-\mathbb{Y}_{k}|^{p}]\leq C(p,T)\Bigg(1+\mathbb{E}\Big|\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}\xi_{j}\Big|^{p}\Bigg)

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
\Phi
be constructible functional with construction
\varphi
and let
\pi:0=t_{0}<t_{t}\cdots<t_{N}=T
be a partition with
\delta t_{i}=t_{i+1}-t_{i}
and
h=\sup_{i}|\delta t_{i}|\leq KN^{-1}
for some
K>0
. We define the following

Appendix A
Proof of auxiliary results

Proof of Lemma
4.7
.

The first assertion follows by induction by noticing that
\delta Z_{i}=\mathbb{E}[\delta Y_{i+1}H_{i}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}]
and by the following standard linearization

\displaystyle\delta Y_{i}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\delta Y_{i+1}+\delta t_{i}\Gamma_{i}\mathbb{E}[\delta Y_{i+1}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}]+\delta t_{i}\Pi_{i}\cdot\delta Z_{i}+\delta t_{i}\delta G_{i}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[(1+\delta t_{i}\Gamma_{i}+\delta t_{i}\Pi_{i}\cdot{H}_{i})\delta Y_{i+1}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\delta t_{i}\delta G_{i}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\right].

On the other hand, the previous representation leads to the desired comparison principle. Specifically, observing that
|\Gamma_{i}|\leq\Lambda_{y}
and
|\Pi_{i}|\leq 2\Lambda_{z}(n)\ell(0)
, where
\ell
is a strictly positive function, we deduce from the assumptions of the lemma that, for all
0\leq i\leq N-1
the following holds

\displaystyle\delta Y_{i}=\mathbb{E}\Big[\Upsilon_{i}\delta Y_{t_{N}}+\sum_{k=i}^{N-1}\delta t_{k}\delta G_{k}\frac{\Upsilon_{i}}{\Upsilon_{k}}\Big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]\geq 0.

This concludes the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
4.8
.

We will compare the solution to the BTZ scheme with the solution
\hat{Y}_{i}
, which has terminal value
\|\xi\|_{\infty}
and drivers
F_{i}
given by

F_{i}(y,z)=\Lambda_{0}+\Lambda_{y}|y|
. SBy induction, we deduce that
\hat{Y}_{i}
is deterministic and the control process given by
\hat{Z}_{i}=\mathbb{E}[\hat{Y}_{i+1}H_{i}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}]=0
. From the assumption, we observe that

G_{i}(\hat{Y}_{i},\hat{Z}_{i})\leq\Lambda_{0}+\Lambda_{y}|\hat{Y}_{i}|=F_{i}(\hat{Y}_{i},0)=F_{i}(\hat{Y}_{i},\hat{Z}_{i}).

This implies that
Y_{i}\leq\hat{Y}_{i}
based on the comparison principle mentioned above. On the other hand, the following holds

\displaystyle\hat{Y}_{i}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(1+\Lambda_{y}\delta t_{i}\Big)\hat{Y}_{i+1}+\Lambda_{0}\delta t_{i}

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{i=0}^{N-1}\Big(1+\Lambda_{y}\delta t_{i}\Big)\|\xi\|_{\infty}+\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\Big\{\prod_{j=0}^{i-1}\Big(1+\Lambda_{y}\delta t_{j}\Big)\Big\}\Lambda_{0}\delta t_{i}

\displaystyle\leq(\|\xi\|_{\infty}+\Lambda_{0}T)\exp\left(1+\Lambda_{y}T\right).

On the other hand, by using a similar argument as above one can obtain that

\hat{Y}_{i}\geq-(\|\xi\|_{\infty}+\Lambda_{0}T)\exp\left(1+\Lambda_{y}T\right).

∎

Proof of Lemma
4.10
.

We will use a technique similar to that employed in
[
8
]
and
[
54
]
. Let
\mathscr{M}
denote the bound of the BTZ scheme
Y_{i}^{\pi}
obtained in Lemma
4.8
. Define
(Y_{i}^{(l),\pi},Z_{i}^{(l),\pi})_{1\leq l\leq\kappa}
as the BTZ scheme associated with the terminal value
\xi^{(l)}=\frac{\xi}{\kappa}
and the following generator.

\displaystyle\hat{G}_{i}^{(l)}(y,z)

\displaystyle=\frac{G_{i}(0,0)}{\kappa}+G_{i}\Big(y+\sum_{m=1}^{l-1}\mathbb{E}[Y_{i+1}^{(m),\pi}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}],z+\sum_{m=1}^{l-1}Z_{i}^{(m),\pi}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad-G_{i}\Big(\sum_{m=1}^{l-1}\mathbb{E}[Y_{i+1}^{(m),\pi}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}],\sum_{m=1}^{l-1}Z_{i}^{(m),\pi}\Big),

(A.1)

with the convention that
\sum_{i=1}^{0}=0
and
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}
not depending on
N
. From uniqueness and linearity of the BTZ scheme, we obtain that

\displaystyle Y_{i}^{\pi}=\sum_{l=1}^{\kappa}Y_{i}^{(l),\pi}\quad\text{ and }\quad Z_{i}^{\pi}=\sum_{l=1}^{\kappa}Z_{i}^{(l),\pi}.

Moreover, for all
y,y^{\prime},z,z^{\prime}
we have that

\displaystyle|\hat{G}_{i}^{(l)}(y,z)-\hat{G}_{i}^{(l)}(y^{\prime},z^{\prime})|

\displaystyle=\Big|G_{i}\Big(y+\sum_{m=1}^{l-1}\mathbb{E}[Y_{i+1}^{(m),\pi}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}],z+\sum_{m=1}^{l-1}Z_{i}^{(m),\pi}\Big)-G_{i}\Big(y^{\prime}+\sum_{m=1}^{l-1}\mathbb{E}[Y_{i+1}^{(m),\pi}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}],z^{\prime}+\sum_{m=1}^{l-1}Z_{i}^{(m),\pi}\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Lambda_{y}(1+|z-z^{\prime}|^{\alpha_{0}})|y-y^{\prime}|+\Lambda_{z}(n)\ell(|y-y^{\prime}|)|z-z^{\prime}|.

This immediately implies that for all
l\in\{1,\cdots,\kappa\}
the family of drivers
(\hat{G}_{i}^{(l)})_{i}
satisfies Assumption
4.4
. Therefore, applying Lemma
4.8
yields that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq i\leq N}|Y_{i}^{(l),\pi}|\leq\Big(\|\xi^{(l)}\|_{\infty}+\frac{|G_{i}(0,0)|}{\kappa}\Big)\exp\left(1+\Lambda_{y}T\right)=\frac{\mathscr{M}}{\kappa}.

(A.2)

It is then sufficient to establish that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}{\delta t_{j}}|Z_{j}^{(l),\pi}|^{2}\Big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]\leq C

(A.3)

for all
l\in\{1,\cdots,\kappa\}
with
C
a constant independent of
N
. We observe that, the BTZ solution
(Y_{i}^{(l),\pi},Z_{i}^{(l),\pi})_{1\leq l\leq\kappa}
can be rewritten as

\displaystyle Y_{i+1}^{(l),\pi}=Y_{i}^{(l),\pi}-G_{i}^{(l),\pi}\big(\mathbb{E}[Y_{i+1}^{(l),\pi}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}],Z_{i}^{(l),\pi}\big)\delta t_{i}+\nu_{i},

(A.4)

where

\displaystyle\nu_{i}=\mathit{B}_{i}-\frac{\delta t_{i}}{c_{i}}Z_{i}^{(l),\pi}\cdot({H}_{i})^{T},\quad\mathit{B}_{i}=Y_{i+1}^{(l),\pi}-\mathbb{E}[Y_{i+1}^{(l),\pi}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}].

Thus squaring both sides of (
A.4
) and using the definition of
\nu_{i}
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\delta t_{i}}{C_{2}}|Z_{i}^{(l),\pi}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}[|Y_{i+1}^{(l),\pi}|^{2}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}]-|Y_{i}^{(l),\pi}|^{2}+2Y_{i}^{(l),\pi}\hat{G}_{i}^{(l)}\left(\mathbb{E}[Y_{i+1}^{(l),\pi}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}],Z_{i}^{(l),\pi}\right)\delta t_{i}.

(A.5)

Using primarily the growth properties of
G_{i}
, we obtain for all
l\in\{1,\cdots,\kappa\}

\displaystyle|\hat{G}_{i}^{(l)}(y,z)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\Lambda_{0}}{\kappa}+\Lambda_{z}+\Lambda_{y}(1+|z|^{\alpha_{0}})|y|+\Lambda_{z}(1+\ell(|y|))|z|^{2}+\Lambda_{z}\ell(|y|)(l-1)\sum_{m=1}^{l-1}|Z_{i}^{(m),\pi}|^{2}.

In particular, using the bound (
A.2
) and the local boundedness of the function
\ell
, we deduce the existence of a constant
C(\|\ell\circ|Y^{(l),\pi}|\|_{\infty})
that does not depend on
N
, such that

\displaystyle|\hat{G}_{i}^{(l)}\left(\mathbb{E}[Y_{i+1}^{(l),\pi}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}],Z_{i}^{(l),\pi}\right)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\Lambda_{0}+\Lambda_{y}\mathscr{M}}{\kappa}+\Lambda_{z}+\frac{\Lambda_{y}\mathscr{M}}{\kappa}|Z_{i}^{(l),\pi}|^{\alpha_{0}}+\Lambda_{z}(1+\|\ell\circ|Y^{(l),\pi}|\|_{\infty})|Z_{i}^{(l),\pi}|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C(\|\ell\circ|Y^{(l),\pi}|\|_{\infty})(l-1)\sum_{m=1}^{l-1}|Z_{i}^{(m),\pi}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\Lambda_{0}+\Lambda_{y}\mathscr{M}}{\kappa}+\Lambda_{z}+C_{\alpha_{0}}\Big(\frac{\Lambda_{y}\mathscr{M}}{\kappa}\Big)^{\frac{2}{2-\alpha_{0}}}+\Lambda_{z}(1+\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2}+\|\ell\circ|Y^{(l),\pi}|\|_{\infty})|Z_{i}^{(l),\pi}|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C(\|\ell\circ|Y^{(l),\pi}|\|_{\infty})(l-1)\sum_{m=1}^{l-1}|Z_{i}^{(m),\pi}|^{2}.

(A.6)

Plugging (
A.6
) into (
A.5
), using an induction argument on
i
and taking the conditional expectation with respect to
\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{1}{2C_{2}}-2\frac{\mathscr{M}}{\kappa}\Lambda_{z}(1+\frac{\alpha_{0}}{2}+\|\ell\circ|Y^{(l),\pi}|\|_{\infty})\Big)\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\delta t_{j}|Z_{j}^{(l),\pi}|^{2}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq 2\frac{\mathscr{M}}{\kappa}\Big(\Big(\frac{\Lambda_{0}}{\kappa}+\Lambda_{z}\Big)T+C(\|\ell\circ|Y^{(l),\pi}|\|_{\infty})(l-1)\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{m=1}^{l-1}\delta t_{j}|Z_{j}^{(m),\pi}|^{2}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+2\frac{\mathscr{M}^{2}}{\kappa^{2}}\Big(1+2\Lambda_{y}T\Big)+2\frac{\mathscr{M}}{\kappa}C_{\alpha_{0}}\Big(\frac{\Lambda_{y}\mathscr{M}}{\kappa}\Big)^{\frac{2}{2-\alpha_{0}}}T.

Hence, an appropriate choice of the parameter
\kappa
ensures the existence of a constant
C
independent of
N
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\delta t_{j}|Z_{j}^{(l),\pi}|^{2}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]\leq C\Big(1+\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{m=1}^{l-1}\delta t_{j}|Z_{j}^{(m),\pi}|^{2}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]\Big).

Therefore (
A.3
) follows from an induction principle on
l
. This concludes the proof. ∎

Proof of Lemma
4.13
.

We first observe that the perturbed scheme (
4.36
) can be rewritten as follows

\displaystyle\tilde{Y}_{i}=\tilde{Y}_{i+1}+\delta t_{i}G_{i}(\mathbb{E}[\tilde{Y}_{i+1}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}],\tilde{Z}_{i})+\zeta_{i}^{Y}-\frac{\delta t_{i}}{c_{i}}(\tilde{Z}_{i})^{\mathbb{T}}\cdot H_{i}-\tilde{\nu}_{i},

(A.7)

where
\tilde{\nu}_{i}
is an
\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i+1}}\text{-measurable}
random variable, such that
\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\nu}_{i}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}]=\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\nu}_{i}H_{i}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}]=0
and
\mathbb{E}[(\tilde{\nu}_{i})^{2}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}]<\infty.

Hence, together with (
4.33
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\tilde{\delta}Y_{i}^{\pi}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\delta}Y_{i+1}^{\pi}+\left(G_{i}(\mathbb{E}[\tilde{Y}_{i+1}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}],\tilde{Z}_{i})-G_{i}(\mathbb{E}[{Y}^{\pi}_{i+1}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}],{Z}_{i}^{\pi})\right)+\zeta_{i}^{Y}-\frac{\delta t_{i}}{c_{i}}(\tilde{\delta}{Z}_{i}^{\pi})\cdot H_{i}-\tilde{\delta}\nu_{i}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\delta}Y_{i+1}^{\pi}+\delta t_{i}\tilde{\Gamma}_{i}\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\delta}{Y}_{i+1}^{\pi}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}]+\delta t_{i}\tilde{\Pi}_{i}\tilde{\delta}{Z}_{i}^{\pi}+\zeta_{i}^{Y}-\frac{\delta t_{i}}{c_{i}}(\tilde{\delta}{Z}_{i}^{\pi})\cdot H_{i}-\tilde{\delta}\nu_{i}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\delta}Y_{i+1}^{\pi}+\mathbb{A}_{i}-\frac{\delta t_{i}}{c_{i}}(\tilde{\delta}{Z}_{i}^{\pi})\cdot H_{i}-\tilde{\delta}\nu_{i},

where
\tilde{\delta}\nu_{i}=\nu_{i}-\tilde{\nu}_{i}
. Using the fact that

\displaystyle(\tilde{\delta}Y_{i+1}^{\pi})^{2}

\displaystyle=(\tilde{\delta}Y_{i}^{\pi})^{2}+2\tilde{\delta}Y_{i}^{\pi}(\tilde{\delta}Y_{i+1}^{\pi}-\tilde{\delta}Y_{i}^{\pi})+(\tilde{\delta}Y_{i+1}^{\pi}-\tilde{\delta}Y_{i}^{\pi})^{2}

\displaystyle=(\tilde{\delta}Y_{i}^{\pi})^{2}+2\tilde{\delta}Y_{i}^{\pi}\Big(\frac{\delta t_{i}}{c_{i}}(\tilde{\delta}{Z}_{i}^{\pi})\cdot H_{i}+\tilde{\delta}\nu_{i}-\mathbb{A}_{i}\Big)+\Big(\frac{\delta t_{i}}{c_{i}}(\tilde{\delta}{Z}_{i}^{\pi})\cdot H_{i}+\tilde{\delta}\nu_{i}-\mathbb{A}_{i}\Big)^{2}.

Then by taking the conditional expectation, and from the assumptions of the Lemma, we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[(\tilde{\delta}Y_{i+1}^{\pi})^{2}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\right]\geq(\tilde{\delta}Y_{i}^{\pi})^{2}-2\delta t_{i}\tilde{\delta}Y_{i}^{\pi}\left(\tilde{\Gamma}_{i}\mathbb{E}[\tilde{\delta}{Y}_{i+1}^{\pi}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}]+\tilde{\Pi}_{i}\tilde{\delta}{Z}_{i}^{\pi}\right)-2\tilde{\delta}Y_{i}^{\pi}(\zeta_{i}^{Y})+\frac{\delta t_{i}}{c_{i}}|\tilde{\delta}{Z}_{i}^{\pi}|^{2}.

Hence, by rearranging the terms above, applying Young’s inequality, summing over
i
and taking the expectation, we deduce the existence of a constant
C
that does not depend on
N
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}(\tilde{\delta}Y_{i}^{\pi})^{2}\delta t_{i}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\mathbb{E}[(\tilde{\delta}Y_{N}^{\pi})^{2}-(\tilde{\delta}Y_{0}^{\pi})^{2}]+\mathbb{E}[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N-1}|\tilde{\delta}Y_{i}^{\pi}|^{2}]+\mathbb{E}\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\tilde{\delta}Y_{i}^{\pi}(\zeta_{i}^{Y})\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\delta t_{i}(\tilde{\delta}Y_{i}^{\pi})^{2}|\tilde{\Pi}_{i}|^{2}\Big]

To conclude, we first recall that
|\tilde{\Pi}_{i}|\leq\Lambda_{z}(1+\ell(0)(|\tilde{Z}_{i}|+|Z_{i}^{\pi}|))\in\mathscr{H}_{BMO}
. Hence from the BDG inequality for discrete martingale, we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\delta t_{i}(\tilde{\delta}Y_{i}^{\pi})^{2}|\tilde{\Pi}_{i}|^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\delta t_{i}|\tilde{\Pi}_{i}|^{2}\Big)^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N-1}|\tilde{\delta}Y_{i}^{\pi}|^{4}]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}[\sup_{0\leq i\leq N-1}|\tilde{\delta}Y_{i}^{\pi}|^{4}]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

The proof is completed.
∎

Proof of Lemma
4.15
.

Let us notice that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}|\tilde{Z}_{i}^{n}|^{2}\delta t_{j}\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}|\tilde{Z}_{j}^{n}-\bar{\bar{Z}}_{j}^{n}|^{2}\delta t_{j}\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]+2\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}|\bar{\bar{Z}}_{j}^{n}|^{2}\delta t_{j}\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]

\displaystyle=2(J_{1}+J_{2}),

with

\displaystyle\bar{\bar{Z}}_{j}^{n}:=\bar{\bar{Z}}^{n}(t)\quad\forall t\in[t_{j},t_{j+1}],\quad\bar{\bar{Z}}_{j}^{n}:=\mathbb{E}\Big[Y_{t_{j+1}}^{n}\frac{W_{t_{j+1}}-W_{t_{j}}}{\delta t_{j}}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}\Big].

(A.8)

We start with the computation of
J_{1}
. We observe that

\displaystyle|\tilde{Z}_{j}-\bar{\bar{Z}}_{j}^{n}|^{2}\delta t_{j}=\mathbb{E}\Big[(Y^{n}_{t_{j+1}}-Y^{n}_{t_{j}})\Big(H_{i}^{R}-\frac{W_{t_{j+1}}-W_{t_{j}}}{\delta t_{j}}\Big)/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}\Big]^{2}\delta t_{j}.

Therefore, using, for instance, the definition of the coefficients
H^{R}_{i}
given in (
1.10
), we obtain the existence of a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}|\tilde{Z}_{j}-\bar{\bar{Z}}_{j}^{n}|^{2}\delta t_{j}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}|Y^{n}_{t_{j+1}}-Y^{n}_{t_{j}}|^{2}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\Big|\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}g_{n}(s,X_{s},Y_{s}^{n},Z_{s}^{n})\mathrm{d}s-\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}Z_{s}^{n}\mathrm{d}W_{s}\Big|^{2}\Big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\int_{t_{i}}^{T}|g_{n}(s,X_{s},Y_{s}^{n},Z_{s}^{n})|\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2}+\int_{t_{i}}^{T}|Z_{s}^{n}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big].

Here, we used the conditional BDG inequality to obtain the last inequality. The second term on the right-hand side can easily be controlled by the
\mathscr{H}_{BMO}\text{-norm}
of the process
Z^{n}
, uniformly in
n
.
Meanwhile, a careful inspection of the first term is crucial, as we do not have an almost sure bound for the process
Z^{n}_{t}
of the form
\sup_{n}|Z_{t}^{n}|\leq C(1+\sup_{t}|X_{t}|)
as seen in
[
8
]
or
[
54
]
, for instance.
Using the growth of
g_{n}
, the uniform bound on the process
Y^{n}
and the local boundedness of the function
\ell
, we observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\int_{t_{i}}^{T}|g_{n}(s,X_{s},Y_{s}^{n},Z_{s}^{n})|\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2}\Big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]\leq CT^{2}+C\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\int_{t_{i}}^{T}|Z_{s}^{n}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2}\Big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big].

Let
h
be the Lyapunov function given in Lemma
4.2
. Applying the Itô’s formula, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}h(Y_{s}^{n})=\Big(\frac{1}{2}h^{\prime\prime}(Y_{s}^{n})|Z_{s}^{n}|^{2}-h^{\prime}(Y_{s}^{n})g_{n}(s,X_{s},Y_{s}^{n},Z_{s}^{n})\Big)\mathrm{d}s+\mathrm{d}M_{s}^{n},

(A.9)

where
\mathrm{d}M_{s}^{n}
is a stochastic integral.
Using the property of the Lyapunov function, we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{t_{i}}^{T}|Z_{s}^{n}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq h(Y_{T}^{n})-h(Y_{t_{i}}^{n})+kT+\int_{t_{i}}^{T}\mathrm{d}M_{s}^{n}.

Squaring and taking the conditional expectation in the above inequality, we deduce that

\displaystyle\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Bigg(\int_{t_{i}}^{T}|Z_{s}^{n}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Bigg)^{2}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\Big[|h(Y_{T}^{n})-h(Y_{t_{i}}^{n})|^{2}+k^{2}T^{2}+\int_{t_{i}}^{T}|Z_{s}^{n}|^{2}\mathrm{d}s\big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq 2C\|Dh\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[|Y_{T}^{n}-Y_{t_{i}}^{n}|^{2}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]+Ck^{2}T^{2}+C\|Z^{n}*W\|_{\operatorname{BMO}}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)(1+\|Z^{n}*W\|_{\operatorname{BMO}}),

where, the last inequality follows from the uniform boundedness on the process
Y^{n}
(see (
1.13
)). We have in particular

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\int_{t_{i}}^{T}|g_{n}(s,X_{s},Y_{s}^{n},Z_{s}^{n})|\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2}\Big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]\leq C(T)(1+\|Z^{n}*W\|_{\operatorname{BMO}}).

(A.10)

Furthermore, there exists a constant
C
that does not depend on
n
, such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}|\tilde{Z}_{j}-\bar{\bar{Z}}_{j}^{n}|^{2}\delta t_{j}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]\leq C(1+\|Z*W\|_{\operatorname{BMO}}).

We now turn to the control of
J_{2}
. We observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}|\bar{\bar{Z}}_{j}^{n}|^{2}\delta t_{j}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}|\hat{Z}_{j}^{n}-\bar{\bar{Z}}_{j}^{n}|^{2}\delta t_{j}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]+2\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}|\hat{Z}_{j}|^{2}\delta t_{j}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big],

where

\displaystyle\hat{Z}_{j}^{n}:=\hat{Z}^{n}(t)\quad\forall t\in[t_{j},t_{j+1}],\quad\hat{Z}_{j}^{n}=\frac{1}{\delta t_{j}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{t_{j}}^{t_{j+1}}Z_{s}^{n}\mathrm{d}s\Big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}\Big].

(A.11)

Similarly to
[
8
, Lemma 2.1]
, by using Jensen’s inequality and the tower property, we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}|\hat{Z}_{j}|^{2}\delta t_{j}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]\leq C(1+\sup_{n}\|Z^{n}*W\|_{\operatorname{BMO}}).

(A.12)

On the other hand, from the definitions of the processes
\hat{Z}^{n}
and
\bar{\bar{Z}}^{n}
, respectively, we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}|\hat{Z}_{j}^{n}-\bar{\bar{Z}}_{j}^{n}|^{2}\delta t_{j}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{t_{j}}^{t{{}_{j+1}}}g_{n}(s,X_{s}.Y_{s}^{n},Z_{s}^{n})\mathrm{d}s\frac{W_{t_{j+1}}-W_{t_{j}}}{\delta t_{j}}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{j}}\Big]\Big|^{2}\delta t_{j}\Big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\int_{t_{i}}^{T}|g_{n}(s,X_{s}.Y_{s}^{n},Z_{s}^{n})|\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2}\Big/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big],

where, the inequality follows from the the Cauchy inequality and the tower property. Therefore, from (
A.10
), there exists a constant independent of
n
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{j=i}^{N-1}|\hat{Z}_{j}^{n}-\bar{\bar{Z}}_{j}^{n}|^{2}\delta t_{j}/\mathfrak{F}_{t_{i}}\Big]\leq C(1+\sup_{n}\|Z^{n}*W\|_{\operatorname{BMO}}).

This concludes the proof.
∎
